# The Gaussian free field limit of the balanced six-vertex model with variance multiplier 1/arcsin(c/2)

Coordinate `EN-115-000-0020-2-2026-PR-T4-UK-EX-M2-C-PH-H-0442` | `PM-225.1` | status ACTIVE | original slug `The-Gaussian-free-field-limit-of-the-balanced-six-vertex-model-with-variance-multiplier-1-over-arcsin-c-over-2-September-23-2026` | date 2026-09-23 | derived from the openai/math preprint The-Gaussian-free-field-limit-of-the-balanced-six-vertex-model-with-variance-multiplier-1-over-arcsin-c-over-2-September-23-2026, modified (Apache-2.0). Publication status: DRAFT.

<!-- PM:BEGIN pm.node.v2 -->
```json
{
  "abstract_verbatim": "We prove that the height function of the square-lattice six-vertex model with weights $`a=b=1`$ and $`0\\lt c\\le2`$, in the plane state obtained from balanced tori, converges to a multiple of the Gaussian free field. For unit height jumps and Green kernel $`-(2\\pi)^{-1}\\log|x-y|`$, the squared multiplier is $`1/\\arcsin(c/2)`$.",
```

<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove that the height function of the square-lattice six-vertex model with weights $`a=b=1`$ and $`0\lt c\le2`$, in the plane state obtained from balanced tori, converges to a multiple of the Gaussian free field. For unit height jumps and Green kernel $`-(2\pi)^{-1}\log|x-y|`$, the squared multiplier is $`1/\arcsin(c/2)`$.

- **claim-main-gff-limit** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): For every fixed $c\in(0,2]$, the prescribed iterated balanced-torus limit $\mathbb P_c$ exists. Define $$\label{eq:multiplier} \Delta=1-\frac{c^2}{2},\qquad \sigma(c)^2=\frac2{\arccos\Delta}=\frac1{\arcsin(c/2)}.$$ As $\delta\downarrow0$ through arbitrary positive real values, the fields $h_\delta$ converge to $\sigma(c)\Gamma$ in each of the following senses.* 1. *For every finite family $\mu_1,\ldots,\mu_m$ of compactly supported finite signed measures of total mass zero and finite energy, $$\bigl(\langle h_\delta,\mu_j\rangle\bigr)_{j=1}^m \ \Longrightarrow\ \mathcal N\!\left(0, \bigl(\sigma(c)^2\mathcal E(\mu_i,\mu_j)\bigr)_{i,j=1}^m\right).$$* 2. *For every bounded open $U\subset\mathbb R^2$ and $-1<\alpha<0$, there is convergence in law, with tightness, in the ordinary restriction spaces $$\mathcal C^\alpha_{\mathrm{res}}(U):=B^\alpha_{\infty,\infty}(U), \qquad B^\alpha_{p,q}(U),\qquad W^{\alpha,p}(U), \quad 1\le p<\infty,\quad 1\le q\le\infty,$$ modulo constants. Convergence also holds in the outward completion <a href="#eq:source-holder" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:source-holder">[eq:source-holder]</a> defined below. No regularity of $\partial U$ is required. One may fix a smooth compactly supported $\rho$ of integral one and replace each field $X$ by $X-\langle X,\rho\rangle$, using the same pinning for the limit.* 3. *For every $k\ge1$ and every tuple $(u_i,u_i')_{i=1}^k$ with $\{u_i,u_i'\}\cap\{u_j,u_j'\}=\varnothing$ for $i\ne j$, $$\label{eq:increment-main} \mathbb E_c\prod_{i=1}^k \bigl(h_\delta(u_i')-h_\delta(u_i)\bigr) \longrightarrow \sigma(c)^k\sum_{\pi}\prod_{\{i,j\}\in\pi}C_{ij},$$ where the sum is over pairings of $\{1,\ldots,k\}$, is zero for odd $k$, and $$C_{ij}=G(u_i',u_j')-G(u_i',u_j)-G(u_i,u_j')+G(u_i,u_j).$$ The convergence is uniform on compact sets of such tuples.
  - Explanation: The plane law exists and the rescaled height converges to a multiple of the free field in the three stated senses.
- **claim-row-algebra** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-row-algebra)): For even $L$, real $b_j$, and all complex spectral arguments, $$\label{eq:monodromy-gauge} M_{\rm ph}(x)=\mathcal P_L M(x)\mathcal P_L\mathcal Q_L, \qquad T^D(x)^*=T^{X\overline D X}(\bar x).$$ The rows with a common diagonal twist commute. More generally, put $$\mathcal R(u)= \begin{pmatrix} [u+i\lambda]/s_\lambda&0&0&0\\ 0&[u]/s_\lambda&1&0\\ 0&1&[u]/s_\lambda&0\\ 0&0&0&[u+i\lambda]/s_\lambda \end{pmatrix},\qquad \mathcal R_{\rm ph}(u)=Z_a\mathcal R(u)Z_b.$$ Then $$\label{eq:physical-RTT} \mathcal R_{{\rm ph},ab}(p-q)M_{{\rm ph},a}(p)M_{{\rm ph},b}(q) =M_{{\rm ph},b}(q)M_{{\rm ph},a}(p) \mathcal R_{{\rm ph},ab}(p-q).$$ Where the intertwiner is invertible, exchanging two rows conjugates their joint auxiliary mark by $\mathcal R_{\rm ph}(p-q)$. For the homogeneous chain, $T^I(i\lambda/2)$ is the cyclic spin translation and $T^I(-i\lambda/2)$ is its inverse.
  - Explanation: Gauge relation, adjoint rule and RTT commutation for marked rows.
- **claim-bethe-data** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-bethe-data)): Let $L$ be even, $b_j$ real, and $0<\lambda<\pi$. A real solution of <a href="#eq:consecutive-counting" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:consecutive-counting">[eq:consecutive-counting]</a> satisfies <a href="#eq:bethe-equations" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:bethe-equations">[eq:bethe-equations]</a>. If its Bethe vector is nonzero, then $\|Y\rangle$ is an eigenvector of $e^{it}\mathsf A(x)+e^{-it}\mathsf L(x)$ with eigenvalue $\tau_t(x;Y)$, and $\mathcal P_L\|Y\rangle$ is an eigenvector of the physical twisted row with eigenvalue $(-1)^n\tau_t(x;Y)$. The Jacobian of <a href="#eq:consecutive-counting" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:consecutive-counting">[eq:consecutive-counting]</a> is $$\label{eq:Gaudin-matrix} G_{jk}=\xi_Y'(y_j)\delta_{jk}+K(y_j-y_k),\qquad \xi_Y'(u)=\sum_{a=0}^{L-1}s(u-b_a)-\sum_{k=1}^nK(u-y_k).$$ If $\det G\ne0$, the roots have a real analytic continuation for real $t$ near the given value, and on that branch $$\label{eq:twist-root-derivative} G\frac{\,\mathrm dY}{\,\mathrm dt}=\frac1\pi\mathbf 1.$$ For $\lambda\ge\pi/2$, $G$ is positive definite at every real root list. Whenever $\xi_Y'(y_j)>0$, write $$\label{eq:Gaudin-weights} \ell_j=\frac1{\xi_Y'(y_j)},\qquad (K\ell)_{jk}=K(y_j-y_k)\ell_k; \qquad G\mathop{\mathrm{diag}}(\ell)=I+K\ell.$$ These assertions concern finite matrices; they give no uniform bound on $(I+K\ell)^{-1}$ as $L$ increases.
  - Explanation: Real counting solutions are Bethe solutions; the Jacobian is the Gaudin matrix.
- **claim-finite-actions** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-finite-actions)): For a finite spectral set $U$, put $E=U\cup\{v'\}$. Then $$\begin{aligned} \mathsf B(v')\|U\rangle&=\|E\rangle,\notag\\ \mathsf A(v')\|U\rangle &=\sum_{p\in E}a(p)\beta(v'-p) \prod_{u\in E\setminus\{p\}}f(u-p) \|E\setminus\{p\}\rangle,\label{eq:A-action}\\ \mathsf L(v')\|U\rangle &=\sum_{p\in E}l(p)\beta(p-v') \prod_{u\in E\setminus\{p\}}f(p-u) \|E\setminus\{p\}\rangle,\label{eq:L-action}\\ \mathsf C(v')\|U\rangle &=\sum_{\substack{p,q\in E\\p\ne q}} a(p)l(q)\beta(v'-p)\beta(q-v')f(q-p) \prod_{u\in E\setminus\{p,q\}}f(u-p)f(q-u) \|E\setminus\{p,q\}\rangle.\label{eq:C-action} \end{aligned}$$ The last sum is over ordered pairs: $p$ belongs to the upper diagonal factor and $q$ to the lower diagonal factor. Thus a word of $m$ entries is an explicit sum over paths that add the new argument at each step and remove zero, one, or two elements. If the final set has the original cardinality, its sets of removed original roots and surviving added arguments have equal size, at most $m$.
  - Explanation: Explicit action of monodromy entries on creation products as sums over removed roots.
- **claim-scalar-product** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-scalar-product)): In the normalization <a href="#eq:standard-monodromy" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:standard-monodromy">[eq:standard-monodromy]</a>, $$\label{eq:Slavnov-exact} S(Y,U)=e^{-int}\left(\prod_{y\in Y}l(y)\right) \frac{\det_{j,k}\partial_{y_j}\tau_t(U_k;Y)} {\det C(Y,U)}.$$ Consequently, if $\det G_Y\ne0$, then $S(Y,Y)\ne0$ and $$\label{eq:Gaudin-norm-exact} S(Y,Y)=(2\pi i s_\lambda)^n \left(\prod_{y\in Y}a(y)l(y)\right) \left(\prod_{j\ne k}f(y_j-y_k)\right)\det G_Y.$$ Suppose $U$ is obtained by replacing the roots indexed by $H=(p_1,\ldots,p_k)$ by $V=(v'_1,\ldots,v'_k)$, in those same slots. Set $$A_Y(p)=a(p)\prod_{y\in Y\setminus\{p\}}f(y-p)\quad(p\in Y).$$ Then the normalized scalar product is exactly $$\label{eq:deleted-scalar-product} \frac{S(Y,U)}{S(Y,Y)} =(-s_\lambda)^{-k} \frac{\prod_{v'\in V}a(v')F_Y(v')}{\prod_{p\in H}A_Y(p)} \frac{\det\bigl((G_Y^{-1}m_{v'}^t)_p\bigr)_{H,V}} {\det\bigl(1/[v'-p]\bigr)_{H,V}}.$$ If the positive weights <a href="#eq:Gaudin-weights" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:Gaudin-weights">[eq:Gaudin-weights]</a> are defined, the last numerator equals $$\label{eq:W-determinant} \det\bigl((G_Y^{-1}m_{v'}^t)_p\bigr)_{H,V} =\left(\prod_{p\in H}\ell_p\right) \det\bigl(W_{v'}(p)\bigr)_{H,V}, \qquad W_{v'}=(I+K\ell)^{-1}m_{v'}^t.$$ Empty products and determinants have value one.
  - Explanation: Scalar products and norms as determinant ratios with deleted columns.
- **claim-hilbert-dual** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-hilbert-dual)): For even $L$ and real inhomogeneities, $$\label{eq:B-Hilbert-adjoint} \mathsf B(x)^*=-\mathsf C(\bar x).$$ Thus for a distinct real on-shell list with $\det G_Y\ne0$, the physical unit vector $$\label{eq:physical-Bethe-vector} \Omega_Y=\frac{\mathcal P_L\|Y\rangle}{\\|\mathcal P_L\|Y\rangle\\|}$$ is well defined, and $$\\|\mathcal P_L\|Y\rangle\\|^2=(-1)^nS(Y,Y)>0.$$ If $Y,Z$ are two such real on-shell lists of the same size, possibly for different twists, then $$\label{eq:two-orientation-overlap} \|\langle\Omega_Y,\Omega_Z\rangle\|^2 =\frac{S(Y,Z)S(Z,Y)}{S(Y,Y)S(Z,Z)}.$$ These identities also hold for real staggered $b_j$, without a Perron interpretation of the vectors.
  - Explanation: Creation entry adjoint is minus annihilation; Bethe vectors have positive norm.
- **claim-finite-word-expansion** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-finite-word-expansion)): Expand each auxiliary mark as $$T^{D_j}=\sum_{\alpha,\beta}(D_j)_{\beta\alpha} M_{{\rm ph},\alpha\beta}.$$ For each resulting entry word, let $n_j$ be the number of minus spins after its $j$th action, with $n_0=n$. Entry words with $n_m\ne n$ have zero expectation in $\Omega_Y$. For a path of a word with $n_m=n$, let $H\subset Y$ be the roots absent at the end and let $V$ be its surviving added arguments, ordered in the same replacement slots. Then $\|H\|=\|V\|=:k\le m$, and its contribution to the normalized physical expectation is $$\label{eq:exact-path-determinant} \left(\prod_{j=1}^m(D_j)_{\beta_j\alpha_j}\right) (-1)^{\sum_{j=1}^m(n_{j-1}-n)} (-s_\lambda)^{-k} \frac{\prod_{j=1}^m\gamma_j} {\prod_{j=1}^m(1+q_Y(v'_j))} \frac{\det(W_{v'}(p))_{H,V}} {\det(1/[v'-p])_{H,V}} \prod_{p\in H}\ell_p.$$ The expectation is the sum of these contributions. In particular, the determinant size is bounded by the word length. For diagonal entry words the parity sign in <a href="#eq:exact-path-determinant" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:exact-path-determinant">[eq:exact-path-determinant]</a> is one. Among distinct removed original roots the correction product contains only reciprocal $f$ factors, with one for each pair. Each removed original root also contributes one factor $\beta$ joining it to the row argument at the step that removes it.
  - Explanation: Word expectation as a sum over paths of determinants of bounded size.
- **claim-root-limits** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-root-limits)): The zero-twist consecutive-root equations of Lemma <a href="#lem:bethe-data" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:bethe-data">3</a> have distinct real solutions with the following properties.* 1. *If $\lambda>\pi/2$, the solutions at half filling exist for every finite homogeneous or real-array chain. Their counting derivative $\rho_L=\xi'$ is positive. If $$p_L(u)=\sum_{j=0}^{L-1}r(u-b_j),$$ then $\rho_L-p_L$ and their integrated discrepancy are uniformly bounded on the entire line.* 2. *In the homogeneous chain, let $$r_Q=(I+K_Q)^{-1}s,\qquad H_Q=(I+K_Q)^{-1}1,$$ where $K_Q$ integrates over $[-Q,Q]$. For all $Q\ge0$, $$\label{eq:regular-density-comparison} c r(u)\le r_Q(u)\le C r(u),\qquad c\le H_Q(u)\le C\quad (\|u\|\le Q).$$ The mass $\int_{-Q}^Q r_Q$ increases to $1/2$, and $$\frac12-\int_{-Q}^Q r_Q\asymp e^{-Q/\eta}.$$ Choose $Q=Q_L$ by $\int_{-Q}^Q r_Q=n/L$. If $Q$ stays bounded with a positive limiting filling, or if $Lr(Q)$ tends to infinity at least as a fixed positive power of $L$, the roots exist for all sufficiently large $L$. If $y_j^0$ are the mid-quantiles of $Lr_Q$, then $Lr_Q(y_j^0)\|y_j-y_j^0\|\le C$ uniformly in $j$, and $$\label{eq:regular-counting-density} \xi'(u)=Lr_Q(u)+O(1)$$ on the interval, including a bounded counting extension at its endpoints.* 3. *In all three limiting procedures the measures $$\nu_L=\sum_j\ell_j\delta_{y_j},\qquad \ell_j=\xi'(y_j)^{-1},$$ have uniformly bounded mass on intervals of length one. In a region whose slightly enlarged neighborhood has density at least $M$, their error against bounded Lipschitz tests on a fixed bounded interval is at most $$\label{eq:local-quadrature} \frac{C}{M}\bigl(\\|\phi\\|_\infty+\mathop{\mathrm{Lip}}(\phi)\bigr).$$ Thus $\nu_L$ converges locally to Lebesgue measure in the dense portions of the limits.* *At zero twist the homogeneous vectors constructed from these roots are the sector Perron vectors. The Gaudin matrices are nonsingular, so each constructed finite root list has a real-analytic twist branch near zero.
  - Explanation: Construction of real roots, density comparison and quadrature estimate.
- **claim-gaudin-inverse** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-gaudin-inverse)): In the limits specified above, $$\label{eq:Gaudin-sup-inverse} \\|(I+K\ell)^{-1}\\|_{\infty\to\infty}\le C,\qquad (K\ell)_{ij}=K(y_i-y_j)\ell_j.$$ There is also a small $a>0$ for which the inverse has uniformly bounded row norm after conjugation by either $e^{ay_i}$ or $e^{-ay_i}$.* *Let $\chi_-,\chi_0,\chi_+$ be a nonnegative partition of unity whose two transitions are translates of fixed smooth profiles with bounded first derivatives. The middle function has compact support, the transitions lie in regions of a fixed sufficiently high density, and the two transition regions recede from one another. The left function is one to the left of its transition and zero to its right; the right function has the opposite convention at the right transition. Put $$D_L=\nu_L-\,\mathrm du,\qquad D^\bullet=\chi_\bullet D_L.$$ The mixed operator $$\label{eq:mixed-operator} I+R_0*(D^0+D^+)$$ has a uniform lower bound in the pointwise sup norm. The analogous assertion holds with the two ends interchanged.
  - Explanation: Uniform sup-norm inverse bounds for Gaudin and mixed operators.
- **claim-row-normalization** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-row-normalization)): There is a real harmonic function $U$ on $\mathcal S_\lambda$ such that, for every finite real-inhomogeneity chain, $$\label{eq:harmonic-row-bound} \\|T^D(x)\\|\le2\\|D\\| \exp\left(\sum_jU(x-b_j)\right).$$ Choose a nonvanishing analytic function $\mathcal N_L$ whose modulus is the exponential on the right. In the homogeneous plane and array limits, the untwisted vacuum eigenvalue $\tau_L$ satisfies $$\label{eq:vacuum-modulus-normalization} \|\tau_L(x)/\mathcal N_L(x)\|\longrightarrow1$$ locally uniformly in the strip, with the moving-bulk convention <a href="#eq:array-limit-convention" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:array-limit-convention">[eq:array-limit-convention]</a> in the array case. Consequently, for each compact $\mathcal K\subset\mathcal S_\lambda$, $$\label{eq:normalized-row-bound} \limsup\sup_{x\in\mathcal K}\\|A_L^D(x)\\|\le2\\|D\\|.$$ In particular the vacuum denominator has no zeros on such a compact for all sufficiently large systems in the indicated sense.
  - Explanation: Harmonic majorant for marked rows and vacuum eigenvalue normalization.
- **claim-weighted-flags** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-weighted-flags)): Let $E=\mathbb C^m$ be the coefficient space of the fixed column list. For each admissible $\beta>0$ there is a subspace $F_{\beta,L}\subset E$ of codimension at most $r_\beta$ such that $$\label{eq:weighted-node-decay} \|W(c)(u)\|\le C_\beta e^{-\beta u}\\|c\\|_2 \quad\hbox{at every positive root }u,\quad c\in F_{\beta,L}.$$ For a fixed finite list of increasing shifts, choose one discrepancy partition for the whole list. The resulting subspaces may be chosen nested. There are analogous flags at the negative end. The constants are uniform in the three limiting procedures, with regular filling understood as $Q\to\infty$ after $L\to\infty$. Restriction to any column coordinate subspace preserves the codimension bound and all unit-vector estimates.
  - Explanation: Nested finite-codimension subspaces with accelerated decay at the roots.
- **claim-joint-tails** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-joint-determinant-tails)): For a fixed generic row word and any one of its nonzero finite action paths, let $H=(p_1,\ldots,p_k)$ be the deleted original roots and $V$ its surviving row arguments. After extracting the quadrature weights $\prod_{i=1}^k\ell_{p_i}$ from <a href="#eq:exact-path-determinant" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:exact-path-determinant">[eq:exact-path-determinant]</a>, the remaining summand is bounded in modulus by $$\label{eq:joint-tail-majorant} C\exp\left(-\varepsilon\sum_{i=1}^k\|p_i\|\right)$$ for some $\varepsilon>0$. The constant and exponent may depend on the fixed word and generic compact parameter set, but are uniform in the limiting procedures. In particular, the sum over paths with $\max_i\|p_i\|>R$ is $O(e^{-\varepsilon'R})$ uniformly, for some $\varepsilon'>0$.
  - Explanation: Exponential decay of path summands in the deleted roots.
- **claim-word-kernel** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-word-kernel)): For each fixed integer $m\ge0$ and fixed $2\times2$ matrices $D_1,\ldots,D_m$, the homogeneous plane coefficients $$\label{eq:word-kernel-definition} \left\langle\Omega_L, A_L^{D_m}(x_m)\cdots A_L^{D_1}(x_1)\Omega_L\right\rangle$$ converge locally uniformly on $\mathcal S_\lambda^m$ to a holomorphic function $\mathcal K_{D_m,\ldots,D_1}(x_m,\ldots,x_1)$. It is invariant under every common complex translation for which the translated arguments remain in the strip. For $\lambda>2\pi/3$, the same functions are the array limits in <a href="#eq:array-limit-convention" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:array-limit-convention">[eq:array-limit-convention]</a>.* *If $v_L$ is any fixed finite linear combination of row words applied to $\Omega_L$, then $$\lim\\|A_L^D(x)v_L\\|^2 \le4\\|D\\|^2\lim\\|v_L\\|^2.$$ The two limits are determined by the word coefficients and the adjoint rule. This is the bound used to construct bounded row actions in Section <a href="#sec:angular" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:angular">4</a>. For $\lambda>2\pi/3$, in the regular-filling procedure the expectations of every fixed equal-time local matrix product converge to those of the homogeneous plane construction.
  - Explanation: Word expectations converge to holomorphic translation-invariant kernels.
- **claim-word-clustering** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-word-clustering)): Partition a fixed finite list of diagonal marked rows into groups. Within each group fix its internal arguments in the strict angle strip. Translate the arguments of group $g$ by a common imaginary amount $it_g$, and let every $\|t_g-t_{g'}\|$ tend to infinity. Then the plane word kernel factors into the product of the individual group kernels. Convergence is locally uniform in the internal strict arguments. The order within each group is retained.
  - Explanation: Separated groups of diagonal rows factorize in the limit.
- **claim-angular-space** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-angular-space)): On $\mathcal H$ the rows act as bounded holomorphic operator families, with $$\\|A^D(z)\\|\le 2\\|D\\|,\qquad A^D(z)^*=A^{X\overline D X}(-\overline z).$$ They satisfy the finite RTT exchange identities wherever their auxiliary intertwiner is invertible. There is a strongly continuous unitary group $U_b$, $b\in\mathbb R$, fixing $\Omega$, such that $$\label{eq:angular-covariance} U_bA^D(z)U_b^*=A^D(z+ib).$$ Words with real arguments in any nonempty open subinterval of $(-v,v)$ span a dense subspace. The family $P=A^I$ consists of commuting normal operators and has a simultaneous representation by almost surely holomorphic scalar functions $f$ on the strip, with $$\label{eq:angular-scalar-star} f(-\overline z)=\overline{f(z)},\qquad \|f(z)\|\le2.$$ In the half-plane coordinate this reality condition reads $f(\overline w)=\overline{f(w)}$.
  - Explanation: Rows act as bounded holomorphic operators with a unitary translation group.
- **claim-scalar-continuation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-scalar-continuation)): There is $r_\lambda>0$, independent of the even chain length, for which the two homogeneous quotients $$T^D(z)T^I(z)^{-1},\qquad T^I(z)^{-1}T^D(z)$$ are holomorphic and bounded by $C_\lambda\\|D\\|$ on discs of radius $r_\lambda$ about $z=v$ and $z=-v$. The limiting rows have strong boundary values $$\label{eq:angular-endpoints} A^D(v)=SD_0,\qquad A^D(-v)=(XD^tX)_0S^{-1},$$ where $S$ is the unitary site shift and $S\Omega=\Omega$. Almost every spectral function extends analytically and without zeros to bands of a common positive width about $\operatorname{Re}z=\pm v$, has modulus one on these lines, and satisfies $\|f\|\le1$ between them. Consequently it is inner in the right half-plane, and every zero $\zeta$ satisfies $$\label{eq:angular-cone} \operatorname{Re}\zeta\ge c_0\|\zeta\|$$ for a fixed $c_0>0$. At each specified strict argument the spectral set on which $f$ vanishes has measure zero.
  - Explanation: Boundary division bound and inner-function continuation of spectral functions.
- **claim-physical-plane** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-physical-plane)): The row representation gives the local probabilities of the plane law $\mathbb P_c$ in Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>. In particular its prescribed iterated balanced-torus limit exists when $c<1$. Quantization with time pointing east and quantization with time pointing north describe this same law. For an ordinary observable supported on one side of either coordinate cut, the squared norm of its prepared state is its expectation paired with its complex-conjugate reflected observable, with the corresponding spin reflection. Products of neutral height observables may be prepared using paths confined to that side.
  - Explanation: Row representation reproduces the plane law in both axial quantizations.
- **claim-inner-spectra** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-inner-spectra)): On $\mathcal D$, almost every spectral function is either $1$ or a finite Blaschke product in the right half-plane, with $$\label{eq:finite-inner-symmetry} f(0)=f(\infty)=1,\qquad f(\overline w)=\overline{f(w)}.$$ Its zeros obey <a href="#eq:angular-cone" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:angular-cone">[eq:angular-cone]</a>. In particular its degree is even; write it as $2n_f$ when it is nonconstant. Every zero produces a fixed-width, fixed-depth dip of $\|f(e^s)\|$ near its logarithmic radius.* *There is a possibly infinite positive measure $\mu$ on the nonconstant functions and a measurable spectral vector $\mathsf h$ such that $$\label{eq:current-cocycle} J(w)\Omega=(1-f(w))\mathsf h, \qquad \mu(\,\mathrm df)=\\|\mathsf h(f)\\|^2\,\sigma(\,\mathrm df).$$ Here $\sigma$ is any scalar measure used for the direct-integral representation; the resulting measure $\mu$ does not depend on that choice. It is invariant under dilation of the argument. On the cross-section where the smallest zero radius is one, it has the form $$\label{eq:orbit-disintegration} \mu=\,\mathrm ds\,\nu(\,\mathrm dF),\qquad f(w)=F(e^s w),\qquad \kappa:=\nu(1)<\infty.$$ If $\mathcal L(F)$ is the difference between its largest and smallest logarithmic zero radii, then $$\label{eq:orbit-width-tail} \nu\{\mathcal L>t\}\le Ce^{-2\eta t}\quad(t\ge1).$$ For a fixed diagonal word $h$, $$\label{eq:diagonal-boost-tail} \\|(1-P(e^s))h\\|\le C_h e^{-\eta\|s\|}.$$ For products of fixed words placed at scales $r_i$, the corresponding bound outside their fixed internal argument ranges is $C\sum_i e^{-\eta\|s-\log r_i\|}$, uniformly when these ranges separate.
  - Explanation: Finite Blaschke spectra and a dilation-invariant current measure.
- **claim-low-band** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-low-band)): There are constants $A,a,C,u_0>0$ such that, for $0<u<u_0$, $Q_uJ(w)Q_u$ extends to a bounded holomorphic function on the full annulus $Au<\|w\|<a/u$. If $$\label{eq:angular-Laurent} Q_uJ(w)Q_u=\sum_{j\in\mathbb Z}w^j J_{u,j},$$ then $\\|J_{u,j}\\|\le C(Cu)^{\|j\|}$, with the exponent zero for $j=0$. Set $\theta=\eta/2>0$. Uniformly on the closed right unit semicircle, $$\begin{aligned} \\|Q_u^\sigma J(w)Q_u^\sigma\\|&\le Cu, &\\|Q_u^\sigma J(w)Q_u^{-\sigma}\\|&\le Cu^\theta, \label{eq:angular-J-bands}\\ Q_u^\sigma N(w)Q_u^\sigma &=\sigma Q_u^\sigma(wJ_{u,1}+w^{-1}J_{u,-1})Q_u^\sigma+O(u^2), \label{eq:angular-N-leading}\\ \\|Q_u^\sigma N(w)Q_u^{-\sigma}\\|&\le Cu^{1+\theta}, &\\|Q_uN(w)Q_u\\|&\le Cu. \label{eq:angular-N-bands} \end{aligned}$$ The estimates remain valid for unequal input and output cutoffs, using their maximum for $u$. With $m$ ordinary time rows on each side, $N$ costs $O(m^{-1})$ and a sign-opposite undoubled $J$ costs $O(m^{-\theta})$. The leading-mode remainder is $O(m^{-1-\theta})$ when, on each dyadic input/output block, the leading expression is taken at the larger cutoff; the precise block sum is defined in the proof.* *Finally, if $S=P(i)$ and $K$ is the principal argument of $S^2$, then $$\label{eq:angular-phase-bound} S^2=e^{iK},\qquad \|K\|\le C H_0.$$
  - Explanation: Low-band annulus extension and sign-block bounds for currents.
- **claim-endpoint-generator** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-endpoint-hamiltonian)): The nonnegative self-adjoint spectral operator $$\label{eq:angular-endpoint-energy} E=S^{-1}\partial_zP(z)\big\|_{z=v},\qquad E(f)=-\left.\partial_s\arg f(ie^s)\right\|_{s=0},$$ agrees on local-spin vectors with the vacuum-subtracted nearest-neighbor Hamiltonian whose link is $$\label{eq:angular-local-link} h_{j,j+1}=-\eta\,\operatorname{swap}_{j,j+1}R'_{j,j+1}(0) =\begin{pmatrix} \eta\cot\lambda&0&0&0\\ 0&0&-\eta/\sin\lambda&0\\ 0&-\eta/\sin\lambda&0&0\\ 0&0&0&\eta\cot\lambda \end{pmatrix}_{j,j+1}.$$ In particular $[h_{j,j+1},Z_j+Z_{j+1}]=0$ and $E\Omega=0$. The closure of the local-spin subspace reduces $E$, and its real-time evolution is the local Hamiltonian evolution. For all sufficiently small $u$, $$\label{eq:angular-E-H-comparison} \mathbf 1_{\{E\le u\}}\le\mathbf 1_{\{H_0\le Cu\}}.$$ With $Z_1=S^{-1}Z_0S$, the exact adjacent-pair identity is $$\label{eq:angular-adjacent-current} N(i)=\tfrac12 S^2(Z_0+Z_1).$$
  - Explanation: Endpoint generator equals the local nearest-neighbour Hamiltonian.
- **claim-sum-rule** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-sum-rule)): Let $p$ denote the phase of the unit shift $S=P(i)$, modulo $2\pi$, and let $\operatorname{sym}$ average a measure at $p$ and $-p$. Set $$D_0=-\langle\Omega,[Z_0,[Z_0,h_{0,1}]]\Omega\rangle.$$ Away from $p=0$, the projection of the current measure obeys $$\label{eq:angular-sum-rule} 2\operatorname{sym}\bigl((p)_*(E\mu)\bigr) =D_0\frac{\,\mathrm dp}{2\pi}.$$ On each orbit of degree $2n_F$, the phase winds monotonically through $n_F$ complete turns. In particular $$\label{eq:angular-degree-integral} \int n_F\,\mathrm d\nu(F)<\infty.$$
  - Explanation: Energy-winding sum rule and finite degree integral.
- **claim-variogram** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-variogram)): After an axial symmetry, any nonzero lattice displacement may be written $(k,j)$ with $k\ge1$ and $\|j\|\le k$. Its height variance is $$\label{eq:angular-variogram} \begin{aligned} \mathbb E_c\bigl(h(k,j)-h(0,0)\bigr)^2 &=2\operatorname{Re}\int\bigl(1-f(1)^k f(\pm i)^{\|j\|}\bigr)\,\mathrm d\mu(f)\\ &=4\kappa\log k+O(1), \end{aligned}$$ where the sign of $i$ is chosen according to the vertical direction. The error is uniform over these displacements. In particular the variance is bounded by $C(1+\log(1+\|k\|+\|j\|))$.
  - Explanation: Height variance is a spectral integral with logarithmic growth.
- **claim-normalization** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-normalization)): With the row and height conventions of Sections <a href="#sec:finite" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:finite">2</a> and <a href="#sec:angular" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:angular">4</a>, $$\label{eq:kappa-value} \kappa=\frac1{2\pi(\pi-\lambda)}.$$ Consequently a limiting covariance $-2\kappa\log\|x-y\|$ has squared multiplier $4\pi\kappa=2/(\pi-\lambda)=\sigma(c)^2$ relative to <a href="#eq:green" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:green">[eq:green]</a>.
  - Explanation: Explicit orbit mass below the first threshold fixes the multiplier.
- **claim-stiffness** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-stiffness)): The iterated homogeneous stiffness satisfies $$\label{eq:stiffness-value} \lim_{Q\to\infty}\lim_{L\to\infty}L e_L''(0) =\frac2{\pi-\lambda}.$$
  - Explanation: Explicit iterated limit of the scaled twist curvature.
- **claim-infrared** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-infrared)): With $\kappa$ as in Section <a href="#sec:angular" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:angular">4</a>, $$\label{eq:kappa-lower} \kappa\ge\frac1{2\pi(\pi-\lambda)}.$$
  - Explanation: Lower bound on the orbit mass from regularized twist curvature.
- **claim-overlap-asymptotic** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-overlap-asymptotic)): In the order $B\to\infty$, then $d\downarrow0$, $$\label{eq:overlap-density} \frac{I_L}{B}\longrightarrow\frac1{2\lambda(\pi-\lambda)}.$$ The limit is understood in the double-limit convention <a href="#eq:array-limit-convention" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:array-limit-convention">[eq:array-limit-convention]</a>.
  - Explanation: Explicit overlap susceptibility density of the staggered array.
- **claim-detector-packing** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-detector-packing)): The plane orbit mass satisfies $$\label{eq:kappa-upper} \kappa\le\frac{\eta}{2\lambda(\pi-\lambda)}.$$
  - Explanation: Upper bound on the orbit mass by packing angular windows.
- **claim-chiral-forms** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-chiral-forms)): There are consistent skew-adjoint forms $j_\pm$ on $\mathcal D_\pm^{\mathrm{fin}}$ such that, for every $R>0$, $$\label{eq:chiral-form-bound} \\|E_\pm(R)j_\pm E_\pm(R)\\|\le CR, \qquad E_\pm(R)j_\pm\Omega=E_\pm(R)a_\pm\mathsf h.$$ Their vacuum means vanish. For $s>0$, the damped forms extend to bounded operators and satisfy $$\label{eq:chiral-damped-form} \\|e^{-sa_\pm}j_\pm e^{-sa_\pm}\\|\le C/s.$$ For every nonnegative measurable function $g$ on $(0,\infty)$, $$\label{eq:chiral-Haar-integral} \int g(a_\pm(f))\,\mathrm d\mu(f) =\kappa\int_0^\infty g(t)\frac{dt}{t}.$$
  - Explanation: Two skew-adjoint chiral current forms with band and damping bounds.
- **claim-chiral-representatives** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-chiral-representatives)): Suppose $T_\delta$ are uniformly bounded operators and, on every finite word tensor, $T_\delta u_\delta$ converges strongly to $Tu$. Then $T$ extends to a bounded operator on the tensor completion, and $T_\delta v_\delta\to Tv$ for every strongly converging family $v_\delta\to v$. Such operator limits may be composed, without a commutation assumption.* *A family $v_\delta$ converges strongly to $v$ if its pairings with all core representatives converge to the pairings with $v$ and its norms converge to $\\|v\\|$.
  - Explanation: Strong core convergence of bounded operators extends and composes.
- **claim-two-scale** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-two-scale)): Take $r_1=\delta^{-1}$ and $r_2=\delta$, with $\delta\downarrow0$ through arbitrary positive real values. On core representatives, the joint spectral functions $(f(\delta^{-1}\,\cdot),f(\delta\,\cdot))$ converge in law in the compact-open topology of the right half-plane to the two tensor-factor functions $(F_1,F_2)$. Jointly with these functions, $$\label{eq:chiral-scaled-spectrum} \begin{split} \delta^{-1}H_0&\longrightarrow A:=a_+\otimes I+I\otimes a_-,\\ \delta^{-1}\arg S&\longrightarrow -a_+\otimes I+I\otimes a_-, \qquad \mathop{\mathrm{sgn}}P(1)\longrightarrow I \end{split}$$ in spectral law. The argument is the principal argument near zero. There is an unbounded set of $R>0$ for which $$\label{eq:chiral-band-limit} Q_\delta(R):=\mathbf 1_{H_0/\delta\le R} \longrightarrow Q(R):=\mathbf 1_{A\le R}$$ strongly on representatives. Bounded spectral multipliers obtained jointly from these limits have the same strong interpretation whenever their discontinuity sets have zero limiting state mass.* *If $m_\delta\ge0$ and $l_\delta$ are integers with $\delta m_\delta\to x\ge0$ and $\delta l_\delta\to y$, then $$\label{eq:chiral-semigroup-limit} P(1)^{m_\delta}S^{l_\delta}\longrightarrow e^{-(x+iy)a_+}\otimes e^{-(x-iy)a_-}$$ strongly on representatives.
  - Explanation: Joint spectral limit splits into two independent chiral factors.
- **claim-current-limit** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-current-limit)): For every admissible $R>0$ in Proposition <a href="#prop:two-scale" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:two-scale">30</a> and every $\|w\|=1$ with $\operatorname{Re}w\ge0$, $$\label{eq:chiral-current-limit} \delta^{-1}Q_\delta(R)N(w)Q_\delta(R) \longrightarrow Q(R)\bigl(w(j_+\otimes I)+w^{-1}(I\otimes j_-)\bigr)Q(R)$$ strongly on representatives. All displayed operators are bounded at fixed $R$. Finite products with propagators from <a href="#eq:chiral-semigroup-limit" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:chiral-semigroup-limit">[eq:chiral-semigroup-limit]</a> converge when successive positive time gaps stay bounded below. In these vacuum products, the cutoffs on both sides of each current may be removed after the scale limit, with error tending to zero uniformly on compact sets of separated time coordinates.
  - Explanation: Rescaled compressed currents converge strongly to chiral currents.
- **claim-chiral-holomorphy** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-chiral-holomorphy)): Let $$U_x=\{(z_1,\ldots,z_k):\operatorname{Re}z_i\ne\operatorname{Re}z_j\text{ for }i\ne j\}, \quad U_y=\{(z_1,\ldots,z_k):\operatorname{Im}z_i\ne\operatorname{Im}z_j\text{ for }i\ne j\}.$$ The function $F_k$ has a symmetric, jointly holomorphic extension to $U_x\cup U_y$, and is the limit of the centered $+$ derivative correlations on both sets.* *Fix $z_2,\ldots,z_k$ with distinct real coordinates and distinct imaginary coordinates. As a function of $z_1$, the extension is holomorphic away from the grid points $$\label{eq:chiral-grid-points} p_{jh}=\operatorname{Re}z_j+i\operatorname{Im}z_h,\qquad 2\le j,h\le k.$$ Near any such point it is $O(\|z_1-p_{jh}\|^{-2})$, locally uniformly as the fixed points vary while retaining these coordinate separations. For $k\ge2$ it tends to zero as $\|z_1\|\to\infty$ with the other points fixed.
  - Explanation: Holomorphic symmetric extension with at most double poles on the grid.
- **claim-collision** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-collision)): For $a=a_+$ and $j=j_+$, the bounded operators $$\label{eq:chiral-collision-operator} K_\epsilon=e^{-\epsilon a}(\epsilon j)e^{-\epsilon a} (\epsilon j)e^{-\epsilon a}$$ are uniformly bounded for $\epsilon>0$ and satisfy $$\label{eq:chiral-collision-limit} K_\epsilon\rightharpoonup-\kappa I \qquad(\epsilon\downarrow0).$$ Consequently, fix $z_2,\ldots,z_k$ with pairwise distinct real coordinates and pairwise distinct imaginary coordinates, and let $2\le j\le k$. Then $$\label{eq:chiral-double-pole} \lim_{\epsilon\downarrow0}\epsilon^2 F_k(z_j+\epsilon,z_2,\ldots,z_k) =-\kappa F_{k-2}(z_2,\ldots,\widehat z_j,\ldots,z_k).$$ The coefficient on the right is independent of both colliding positions.
  - Explanation: Adjacent chiral currents collapse to a scalar double-pole coefficient.
- **claim-gaussian-currents** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-gaussian-currents)): Let $k\ge1$, and let $z_1,\ldots,z_k$ have pairwise distinct real coordinates or pairwise distinct imaginary coordinates. For $\varepsilon_i\in\{+,-\}$ and moving mesh faces $z_{i,\delta}\to z_i$, $$\label{eq:chiral-Gaussian-current-limit} \lim_{\delta\downarrow0} \mathbb E\prod_{i=1}^k \delta^{-1}D_\delta^{\varepsilon_i}h_\delta(z_{i,\delta}) =\sum_{\pi}\ \prod_{\{i,j\}\in\pi} C_{\varepsilon_i,\varepsilon_j}(z_i,z_j),$$ where the sum is over pairings, is zero for odd $k$, and $$\label{eq:chiral-contractions} C_{++}(z,\xi)=-\frac{\kappa}{(z-\xi)^2},\qquad C_{--}(z,\xi)=-\frac{\kappa}{(\bar z-\bar\xi)^2},\qquad C_{+-}(z,\xi)=C_{-+}(z,\xi)=0.$$ Convergence is uniform on compact subsets of either separated-time region. By linearity the corresponding assertion holds for all products of the real centered derivatives $\delta^{-1}D_{x,\delta}$ and $\delta^{-1}D_{y,\delta}$. These are the off-diagonal derivatives of the Gaussian covariance $$\label{eq:chiral-log-covariance} K_\kappa(z,\xi)=-2\kappa\log\|z-\xi\|.$$
  - Explanation: Wick pairings with explicit chiral contractions.
- **claim-ordinary-moments** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-ordinary-moments)): Fix $0<A<\infty$. Let $R\ge1$, and let $g=(g_l)_{l\in\mathbb Z}$ be a finitely supported real sequence such that $$\label{eq:prob-g-conditions} \sum_l\|g_l\|\le AR,\qquad \sum_l\|g_{l+1}-g_l\|\le A,\qquad \sum_l\|g_{l+1}-g_l\|^2\le A/R.$$ Then, for every $1\le b<\infty$, $$\label{eq:prob-ordinary-moments} \left\\|\sum_lg_lZ_l\right\\|_{L^b(\mathbb P_c)}\le C_{A,b}.$$ In particular the conclusion holds if $g$ has support of length at most $AR$, $\|g_l\|\le A$, and $\|g_{l+1}-g_l\|\le A/R$. The same assertions hold with the two axes interchanged.
  - Explanation: Uniform moments of weighted column spin sums.
- **claim-neutral-blocks** (bound, [source](#stmt-cor-neutral-blocks)): Let $b\in\mathbb R^2$, $r>0$, and let $f$ be a measurable real density supported in a square of side $Ar$ centered within distance $Ar$ of $b$, with $$\int f=0,\qquad \|f\|\le A r^{-2}.$$ For every finite $q$, $$\label{eq:prob-neutral-block} \\|\langle h_\delta,f\rangle\\|_{L^q}\le C_{A,q}$$ uniformly in $b,r,\delta$. Products of finitely many such blocks have uniform absolute moment bounds, even when their supports overlap.
  - Explanation: Uniform moments for neutral density blocks.
- **claim-smooth-tests** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-smooth-tests)): For every finite family of smooth compactly supported densities $\phi_1,\ldots,\phi_m$ of integral zero, the vector $\bigl(\langle h_\delta,\phi_i\rangle\bigr)_i$ converges jointly in law and in every joint moment to the corresponding pairings of $\mathcal H_\kappa$. The moment convergence is uniform when the tests vary in a compact smooth family with a common compact support.
  - Explanation: Smooth neutral pairings converge jointly with all moments.
- **claim-separated-block** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-separated-block)): Fix an integer $k\ge2$, constants $A,c_0,c_1>0$, a separation $d_0>0$, and a bounded region containing all factor centers. Choose $r_0=a_kd_0$, with $a_k>0$ sufficiently small in terms of these fixed data. Let $n\le k$ and let $$A_i=\langle h_\delta,\mu_i\rangle,\qquad 1\le i\le n,$$ where each $\mu_i$ is a finite signed measure of mass zero, total variation at most $A$, and support in a square of side $Ar_i$ about $b_i$. Assume $\\|A_i\\|_{L^{2k}}\le A$. These hypotheses include the neutral density blocks of Corollary <a href="#cor:neutral-blocks" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cor:neutral-blocks">36</a> and the terminal residuals just defined.* *Suppose one selected factor has scale $c_0\delta\le r\le r_0$, all other scales lie in $[r,r_0]$, and all other centers are at distance at least $c_1d_0$ from its center. Then, for some $\tau>0$, $$\label{eq:prob-separated-block} \left\|\mathbb E\prod_{i=1}^n A_i\right\| \le C(r/d_0)^\tau.$$ The constants are uniform in the centers, the measures, the mesh and the scales subject to these bounds. The other centers need not be mutually separated.
  - Explanation: A small separated factor gives a power-law small correlation.
- **claim-offdiagonal** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-offdiagonal)): For every $k\ge1$, all $k$-point height-increment moments converge to the Wick increment moments of $\mathcal H_\kappa$, uniformly on compact sets of tuples whose endpoint sets belonging to different increments are disjoint.
  - Explanation: Separated increment moments converge to Wick moments.
- **claim-log-integrability** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-log-integrability)): Let $\mu$ be a compactly supported finite signed measure for which $\iint G(x,y)\,\mu(dx)\mu(dy)$ is an ordinary finite signed-measure integral. Then $\mu$ has no atoms, and $$\label{eq:prob-absolute-energy} \iint\log_+\frac1{\|x-y\|}\,\|\mu\|(dx)\|\mu\|(dy)<\infty.$$ If $\mu,\nu$ both satisfy this assumption, the corresponding mixed absolute logarithmic integral is finite as well.
  - Explanation: Finite-energy measures are atomless with finite absolute logarithmic energy.
- **claim-finite-energy** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-finite-energy)): For every finite family of compactly supported, neutral, finite signed measures of the ordinary finite-energy class in Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>, the associated height pairings converge jointly in law to the centered Gaussian vector with covariance obtained by integrating $K_\kappa$ against the pairs of measures.
  - Explanation: Finite-energy neutral pairings converge jointly to the Gaussian vector.
- **claim-pinned-averages** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-pinned-averages)): Let $0<\ell\le1$, and let $\psi_{b,\ell}$ be supported in a square of side $A\ell$ about $b$, with $\|\psi_{b,\ell}\|\le A\ell^{-2}$. For centers $b$ in a fixed compact set and every finite $q$, $$\label{eq:prob-pinned-averages} \\|\langle X_\delta^\chi,\psi_{b,\ell}\rangle\\|_{L^q} \le C_{A,q,\chi,\rho}(1+\|\log\ell\|).$$ The estimate is uniform in $\delta>0$.
  - Explanation: Logarithmic moment bound for pinned local averages.
- **claim-negative-regularity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-negative-regularity)): For every bounded open $U\subset\mathbb R^2$ and every $-1<\alpha<0$, the pinned fields converge in law, with tightness, in the ordinary restriction spaces $$\mathcal C^\alpha_{\mathrm{res}}(U):=B^\alpha_{\infty,\infty}(U),\qquad B^\alpha_{p,q}(U),\qquad W^{\alpha,p}(U), \quad 1\le p<\infty,\quad1\le q\le\infty,$$ to the corresponding pinning of $\mathcal H_\kappa$. Here the Hölder laws are supported on the separable closure of smooth functions in the displayed norm. Convergence also holds in the literal outward-scaling completion defined by (16) of (Duminil-Copin, Kozlowski, Lammers, et al. 2026, Definition 2.6(iii)) [[DKLM2026](#ref-DKLM2026)]. No boundary regularity of $U$ is assumed. An intrinsic dual interpretation of the negative Sobolev norms is treated at the end of this section.
  - Explanation: Tight convergence in negative restriction spaces and the outward completion.
- **claim-cell-averages** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-cell-averages)): Let $Q$ be a square of side $0<\ell\le1$ in a fixed bounded box, and let $A\subset Q$ be measurable with $\|A\|>0$. Put $t=\|A\|/\ell^2$. Then for every finite $b$, $$\label{eq:prob-cell-average} \left\\|\frac1{\|A\|}\int_A X_\delta(x)\,dx\right\\|_{L^b} \le C_b(1+\|\log\ell\|+\|\log t\|)$$ uniformly in $\delta$ and in the shape of $A$.
  - Explanation: Moment bound for averages over small-area cells, uniform in shape.
- **claim-intrinsic-dual** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-intrinsic-dual)): Let $U\subset\mathbb R^2$ be bounded and open, let $0<s<1$, and let $1\le p<\infty$, with conjugate exponent $q=p'$. The pinned fields converge in law, with norm tightness, in $(W^{s,q}(U))^*$ to the canonically pinned Gaussian field with kernel $K_\kappa$. The laws are supported on a separable closed subspace of this dual. No extension property or regularity of $\partial U$ is required.
  - Explanation: Tight convergence in intrinsic dual Sobolev spaces on any bounded open set.
- **claim-besov-dual-remark** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-auto-remark-1)): If a negative Besov convention is dual to a positive intrinsic Besov difference norm, choose $0<t<s<1$. The positive test norm of order $s$ controls the intrinsic $W^{t,q}$ norm: in a dyadic difference expansion, the gain $2^{-j(s-t)}$ is summable, for every sequence exponent including its endpoints. The convergence just proved for $(W^{t,q}(U))^*$ therefore gives the corresponding negative Besov dual convergence of order $-s$. This is in addition to the ordinary restriction Besov conclusion of Proposition <a href="#prop:negative-regularity" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:negative-regularity">43</a>.
  - Explanation: Intrinsic Besov dual convergence follows from the Sobolev dual one.
- **claim-endpoint-topologies** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-endpoint-topologies)): At $c=2$, suppose that the height pairings with every finite family of ordinary finite-energy neutral measures converge jointly to those of $\sigma\Gamma$. Then the commonly pinned fields also converge, with tightness, in all the negative-regularity spaces stated in Propositions <a href="#prop:negative-regularity" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:negative-regularity">43</a> and <a href="#prop:intrinsic-dual" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:intrinsic-dual">45</a>. This includes the ordinary restriction Hölder, Besov and Sobolev spaces on every bounded open set, the outward-scaling completion, and the explicitly defined intrinsic Sobolev duals.
  - Explanation: At the endpoint, finite-energy convergence implies all negative topologies.
- **claim-rr-rotation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-rr-rotation)): For every even $k\ge2$ and every compact $K\subset\mathcal D_k$, $$\lim_{\delta\downarrow0} \sup_{\boldsymbol u\in K}\sup_{I\in\operatorname{Isom}(\mathbb R^2)} \left\|\Phi_{k,\delta}(\boldsymbol u) -\Phi_{k,\delta}(I\boldsymbol u)\right\|=0. \label{eq:rr-target}$$
  - Explanation: Asymptotic isometry invariance of fair-signed endpoint correlations.
- **claim-rr-bkw** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-rr-bkw)): After the fixed similarity identifying the medial square grid with $\mathbb Z^2$, the fair unit-jump height of critical plane $\mathrm{FK}_4$ has the same gradient law as the zero-slope six-vertex height with $\mathbf a=\mathbf b=1$ and $\mathbf c=2$.
  - Explanation: Fair loop height has the gradient law of the endpoint six-vertex height.
- **claim-rr-estimates** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-rr-estimates)): Let $N(a,M)$ be the number of loops of diameter at least $a$ meeting $B(0,M)$. For $\delta$ sufficiently smaller than $a$, and every fixed $p<\infty$, $$\\|N(a,M)\\|_{L^p}\le C_p(1+M/a)^2. \label{eq:rr-count}$$ There are $C,\gamma>0$ such that, with harmless fixed changes of the radii, $$\begin{aligned} \mathbb P\{\exists L:L\cap B(0,M)\ne\varnothing, L\not\subset B(0,R)\} &\le C(M/R)^\gamma, && R\ge10M, \label{eq:rr-escape}\\ \mathbb P\{\exists L:\mathop{\mathrm{diam}}L\ge a, \mathop{\mathrm{dist}}(z,L)\le r\} &\le C\bigl((r+C\delta)/a\bigr)^\gamma, &&0<r<a/100. \label{eq:rr-point} \end{aligned}$$ The constants are independent of the deterministic center $z$.
  - Explanation: Loop count, escape and point-avoidance bounds.
- **claim-rr-nails** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-rr-nails)): Fix $\alpha\in(0,\pi)$. As the mesh tends to zero, the construction of (Duminil-Copin, Kozlowski, Krachun, et al. 2026, sec. 3.6) [[DCKKMO2026Rotation](#ref-DCKKMO2026Rotation)] gives a coupling of two auxiliary loop samples. Their restrictions to a deterministic window exhausting the plane have, unconditionally, the respective homogeneous laws on $\delta\mathbb L(\pi/2)$ and $\delta\mathbb L(\alpha)$.* *Outside an event of probability tending to zero, there are correspondingly indexed, pairwise disjoint marked clusters, called nails, whose mesh tends to zero and whose window exhausts the plane. Corresponding nails have diameter tending to zero and lie at distance tending to zero from the same grid point. There is a bijection $\psi$ between all loops surrounding at least two but not all nails in the two samples. It preserves their oriented reduced crossing words relative to the nail grid.
  - Explanation: Homotopy coupling preserving crossing words of nontrivial loops.
- **claim-endpoint-completion** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-endpoint-completion)): The conclusions of Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> hold at $c=2$, with squared multiplier $2/\pi$.
  - Explanation: Main conclusions hold at the endpoint with the stated multiplier.

```json
{
  "claims": [
    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-thm-main",
        "exact_quote": "For every fixed $c\\in(0,2]$, the prescribed iterated balanced-torus limit $\\mathbb P_c$ exists. Define $$\\label{eq:multiplier}\n \\Delta=1-\\frac{c^2}{2},\\qquad\n \\sigma(c)^2=\\frac2{\\arccos\\Delta}=\\frac1{\\arcsin(c/2)}.$$ As $\\delta\\downarrow0$ through arbitrary positive real values, the fields $h_\\delta$ converge to $\\sigma(c)\\Gamma$ in each of the following senses.*\n\n1.  *For every finite family $\\mu_1,\\ldots,\\mu_m$ of compactly supported finite signed measures of total mass zero and finite energy, $$\\bigl(\\langle h_\\delta,\\mu_j\\rangle\\bigr)_{j=1}^m\n     \\ \\Longrightarrow\\\n     \\mathcal N\\!\\left(0,\n           \\bigl(\\sigma(c)^2\\mathcal E(\\mu_i,\\mu_j)\\bigr)_{i,j=1}^m\\right).$$*\n\n2.  *For every bounded open $U\\subset\\mathbb R^2$ and $-1<\\alpha<0$, there is convergence in law, with tightness, in the ordinary restriction spaces $$\\mathcal C^\\alpha_{\\mathrm{res}}(U):=B^\\alpha_{\\infty,\\infty}(U),\n     \\qquad B^\\alpha_{p,q}(U),\\qquad W^{\\alpha,p}(U),\n     \\quad 1\\le p<\\infty,\\quad 1\\le q\\le\\infty,$$ modulo constants. Convergence also holds in the outward completion <a href=\"#eq:source-holder\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:source-holder\">[eq:source-holder]</a> defined below. No regularity of $\\partial U$ is required. One may fix a smooth compactly supported $\\rho$ of integral one and replace each field $X$ by $X-\\langle X,\\rho\\rangle$, using the same pinning for the limit.*\n\n\n3.  *For every $k\\ge1$ and every tuple $(u_i,u_i')_{i=1}^k$ with $\\{u_i,u_i'\\}\\cap\\{u_j,u_j'\\}=\\varnothing$ for $i\\ne j$, $$\\label{eq:increment-main}\n     \\mathbb E_c\\prod_{i=1}^k\n        \\bigl(h_\\delta(u_i')-h_\\delta(u_i)\\bigr)\n     \\longrightarrow\n     \\sigma(c)^k\\sum_{\\pi}\\prod_{\\{i,j\\}\\in\\pi}C_{ij},$$ where the sum is over pairings of $\\{1,\\ldots,k\\}$, is zero for odd $k$, and $$C_{ij}=G(u_i',u_j')-G(u_i',u_j)-G(u_i,u_j')+G(u_i,u_j).$$ The convergence is uniform on compact sets of such tuples.",

          "exact_quote": "For every fixed $c\\in(0,2]$, the prescribed iterated balanced-torus limit $\\mathbb P_c$ exists. Convergence also holds in the outward completion <a href=\"#eq:source-holder\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:source-holder\">[eq:source-holder]</a> defined below.         "anchor": "stmt-lem-row-algebra",
        "exact_quote": "For even $L$, real $b_j$, and all complex spectral arguments, $$\\label{eq:monodromy-gauge}\n M_{\\rm ph}(x)=\\mathcal P_L M(x)\\mathcal P_L\\mathcal Q_L,\n \\qquad\n T^D(x)^*=T^{X\\overline D X}(\\bar x).$$ The rows with a common diagonal twist commute. More generally, put $$\\mathcal R(u)=\n \\begin{pmatrix}\n [u+i\\lambda]/s_\\lambda&0&0&0\\\\\n 0&[u]/s_\\lambda&1&0\\\\\n 0&1&[u]/s_\\lambda&0\\\\\n 0&0&0&[u+i\\lambda]/s_\\lambda\n \\end{pmatrix},\\qquad\n \\mathcal R_{\\rm ph}(u)=Z_a\\mathcal R(u)Z_b.$$ Then $$\\label{eq:physical-RTT}\n \\mathcal R_{{\\rm ph},ab}(p-q)M_{{\\rm ph},a}(p)M_{{\\rm ph},b}(q)\n =M_{{\\rm ph},b}(q)M_{{\\rm ph},a}(p)\n   \\mathcal R_{{\\rm ph},ab}(p-q).$$ Where the intertwiner is invertible, exchanging two rows conjugates their joint auxiliary mark by $\\mathcal R_{\\rm ph}(p-q)$. For the homogeneous chain, $T^I(i\\lambda/2)$ is the cyclic spin translation and $T^I(-i\\lambda/2)$ is its inverse.",
        "source_id": "PM-225.1"
      },
      "evidence": [

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-bethe-data",
        "exact_quote": "Let $L$ be even, $b_j$ real, and $0<\\lambda<\\pi$. A real solution of <a href=\"#eq:consecutive-counting\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:consecutive-counting\">[eq:consecutive-counting]</a> satisfies <a href=\"#eq:bethe-equations\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:bethe-equations\">[eq:bethe-equations]</a>. If its Bethe vector is nonzero, then $|Y\\rangle$ is an eigenvector of $e^{it}\\mathsf A(x)+e^{-it}\\mathsf L(x)$ with eigenvalue $\\tau_t(x;Y)$, and $\\mathcal P_L|Y\\rangle$ is an eigenvector of the physical twisted row with eigenvalue $(-1)^n\\tau_t(x;Y)$. The Jacobian of <a href=\"#eq:consecutive-counting\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:consecutive-counting\">[eq:consecutive-counting]</a> is $$\\label{eq:Gaudin-matrix}\n G_{jk}=\\xi_Y'(y_j)\\delta_{jk}+K(y_j-y_k),\\qquad\n \\xi_Y'(u)=\\sum_{a=0}^{L-1}s(u-b_a)-\\sum_{k=1}^nK(u-y_k).$$ If $\\det G\\ne0$, the roots have a real analytic continuation for real $t$ near the given value, and on that branch $$\\label{eq:twist-root-derivative}\n G\\frac{\\,\\mathrm dY}{\\,\\mathrm dt}=\\frac1\\pi\\mathbf 1.$$ For $\\lambda\\ge\\pi/2$, $G$ is positive definite at every real root list. Whenever $\\xi_Y'(y_j)>0$, write $$\\label{eq:Gaudin-weights}\n \\ell_j=\\frac1{\\xi_Y'(y_j)},\\qquad\n (K\\ell)_{jk}=K(y_j-y_k)\\ell_k;\n \\qquad G\\mathop{\\mathrm{diag}}(\\ell)=I+K\\ell.$$ These assertions concern finite matrices; they give no uniform bound on $(I+K\\ell)^{-1}$ as $L$ increases.",
        "source_id": "PM-225.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-bethe-data",

Whenever $\\xi_Y'(y_j)>0$, write $$\\label{eq:Gaudin-weights}\n \\ell_j=\\frac1{\\xi_Y'(y_j)},\\qquad\n (K\\ell)_{jk}=K(y_j-y_k)\\ell_k;\n \\qquad G\\mathop{\\mathrm{diag}}(\\ell)=I+K\\ell.$$ These assertions concern finite matrices; they give no uniform bound on $(I+K\\ell)^{-1}$ as $L$ increases.",

Whenever $\\xi_Y'(y_j)>0$, write $$\\label{eq:Gaudin-weights}\n \\ell_j=\\frac1{\\xi_Y'(y_j)},\\qquad\n (K\\ell)_{jk}=K(y_j-y_k)\\ell_k;\n \\qquad G\\mathop{\\mathrm{diag}}(\\ell)=I+K\\ell.$$ These assertions concern finite matrices; they give no uniform bound on $(I+K\\ell)^{-1}$ as $L$ increases.",

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-finite-actions",
        "exact_quote": "For a finite spectral set $U$, put $E=U\\cup\\{v'\\}$. Then $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\mathsf B(v')|U\\rangle&=|E\\rangle,\\notag\\\\\n \\mathsf A(v')|U\\rangle\n &=\\sum_{p\\in E}a(p)\\beta(v'-p)\n          \\prod_{u\\in E\\setminus\\{p\\}}f(u-p)\n             |E\\setminus\\{p\\}\\rangle,\\label{eq:A-action}\\\\\n \\mathsf L(v')|U\\rangle\n &=\\sum_{p\\in E}l(p)\\beta(p-v')\n          \\prod_{u\\in E\\setminus\\{p\\}}f(p-u)\n             |E\\setminus\\{p\\}\\rangle,\\label{eq:L-action}\\\\\n \\mathsf C(v')|U\\rangle\n &=\\sum_{\\substack{p,q\\in E\\\\p\\ne q}}\n       a(p)l(q)\\beta(v'-p)\\beta(q-v')f(q-p)\n       \\prod_{u\\in E\\setminus\\{p,q\\}}f(u-p)f(q-u)\n             |E\\setminus\\{p,q\\}\\rangle.\\label{eq:C-action}\n\\end{aligned}$$ The last sum is over ordered pairs: $p$ belongs to the upper diagonal factor and $q$ to the lower diagonal factor. Thus a word of $m$ entries is an explicit sum over paths that add the new argument at each step and remove zero, one, or two elements. If the final set has the original cardinality, its sets of removed original roots and surviving added arguments have equal size, at most $m$.",
        "source_id": "PM-225.1"
      },
      "evidence": [

          "anchor": "stmt-lem-finite-actions",

Thus a word of $m$ entries is an explicit sum over paths that add the new argument at each step and remove zero, one, or two elements.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-scalar-product",
        "exact_quote": "In the normalization <a href=\"#eq:standard-monodromy\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:standard-monodromy\">[eq:standard-monodromy]</a>, $$\\label{eq:Slavnov-exact}\n S(Y,U)=e^{-int}\\left(\\prod_{y\\in Y}l(y)\\right)\n \\frac{\\det_{j,k}\\partial_{y_j}\\tau_t(U_k;Y)}\n      {\\det C(Y,U)}.$$ Consequently, if $\\det G_Y\\ne0$, then $S(Y,Y)\\ne0$ and $$\\label{eq:Gaudin-norm-exact}\n S(Y,Y)=(2\\pi i s_\\lambda)^n\n       \\left(\\prod_{y\\in Y}a(y)l(y)\\right)\n       \\left(\\prod_{j\\ne k}f(y_j-y_k)\\right)\\det G_Y.$$ Suppose $U$ is obtained by replacing the roots indexed by $H=(p_1,\\ldots,p_k)$ by $V=(v'_1,\\ldots,v'_k)$, in those same slots. Set $$A_Y(p)=a(p)\\prod_{y\\in Y\\setminus\\{p\\}}f(y-p)\\quad(p\\in Y).$$ Then the normalized scalar product is exactly $$\\label{eq:deleted-scalar-product}\n \\frac{S(Y,U)}{S(Y,Y)}\n =(-s_\\lambda)^{-k}\n   \\frac{\\prod_{v'\\in V}a(v')F_Y(v')}{\\prod_{p\\in H}A_Y(p)}\n   \\frac{\\det\\bigl((G_Y^{-1}m_{v'}^t)_p\\bigr)_{H,V}}\n        {\\det\\bigl(1/[v'-p]\\bigr)_{H,V}}.$$ If the positive weights <a href=\"#eq:Gaudin-weights\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:Gaudin-weights\">[eq:Gaudin-weights]</a> are defined, the last numerator equals $$\\label{eq:W-determinant}\n \\det\\bigl((G_Y^{-1}m_{v'}^t)_p\\bigr)_{H,V}\n =\\left(\\prod_{p\\in H}\\ell_p\\right)\n       \\det\\bigl(W_{v'}(p)\\bigr)_{H,V},\n \\qquad W_{v'}=(I+K\\ell)^{-1}m_{v'}^t.$$ Empty products and determinants have value one.",
        "source_id": "PM-225.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-scalar-product",
          "exact_quote": "In the normalization <a href=\"#eq:standard-monodromy\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:standard-monodromy\">[eq:standard-monodromy]</a>, $$\\label{eq:Slavnov-exact}\n S(Y,U)=e^{-int}\\left(\\prod_{y\\in Y}l(y)\\right)\n \\frac{\\det_{j,k}\\partial_{y_j}\\tau_t(U_k;Y)}\n      {\\det C(Y,U)}.$$ Consequently, if $\\det G_Y\\ne0$, then $S(Y,Y)\\ne0$ and $$\\label{eq:Gaudin-norm-exact}\n S(Y,Y)=(2\\pi i s_\\lambda)^n\n       \\left(\\prod_{y\\in Y}a(y)l(y)\\right)\n       \\left(\\prod_{j\\ne k}f(y_j-y_k)\\right)\\det G_Y.$$ Suppose $U$ is obtained by replacing the roots indexed by $H=(p_1,\\ldots,p_k)$ by $V=(v'_1,\\ldots,v'_k)$, in those same slots. Set $$A_Y(p)=a(p)\\prod_{y\\in Y\\setminus\\{p\\}}f(y-p)\\quad(p\\in Y).$$ Then the normalized scalar product is exactly $$\\label{eq:deleted-scalar-product}\n \\frac{S(Y,U)}{S(Y,Y)}\n =(-s_\\lambda)^{-k}\n   \\frac{\\prod_{v'\\in V}a(v')F_Y(v')}{\\prod_{p\\in H}A_Y(p)}\n   \\frac{\\det\\bigl((G_Y^{-1}m_{v'}^t)_p\\bigr)_{H,V}}\n        {\\det\\bigl(1/[v'-p]\\bigr)_{H,V}}.$$ If the positive weights <a href=\"#eq:Gaudin-weights\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:Gaudin-weights\">[eq:Gaudin-weights]</a> are defined, the last numerator equals $$\\label{eq:W-determinant}\n \\det\\bigl((G_Y^{-1}m_{v'}^t)_p\\bigr)_{H,V}\n =\\left(\\prod_{p\\in H}\\ell_p\\right)\n       \\det\\bigl(W_{v'}(p)\\bigr)_{H,V},\n \\qquad W_{v'}=(I+K\\ell)^{-1}m_{v'}^t.$$ Empty products and determinants have value one.",
          "source_id": "PM-225.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "In the normalization <a href=\"#eq:standard-monodromy\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:standard-monodromy\">[eq:standard-monodromy]</a>, $$\\label{eq:Slavnov-exact}\n S(Y,U)=e^{-int}\\left(\\prod_{y\\in Y}l(y)\\right)\n \\frac{\\det_{j,k}\\partial_{y_j}\\tau_t(U_k;Y)}\n      {\\det C(Y,U)}.$$ Consequently, if $\\det G_Y\\ne0$, then $S(Y,Y)\\ne0$ and $$\\label{eq:Gaudin-norm-exact}\n S(Y,Y)=(2\\pi i s_\\lambda)^n\n       \\left(\\prod_{y\\in Y}a(y)l(y)\\right)\n       \\left(\\prod_{j\\ne k}f(y_j-y_k)\\right)\\det G_Y.$$ Suppose $U$ is obtained by replacing the roots indexed by $H=(p_1,\\ldots,p_k)$ by $V=(v'_1,\\ldots,v'_k)$, in those same slots. Set $$A_Y(p)=a(p)\\prod_{y\\in Y\\setminus\\{p\\}}f(y-p)\\quad(p\\in Y).$$ Then the normalized scalar product is exactly $$\\label{eq:deleted-scalar-product}\n \\frac{S(Y,U)}{S(Y,Y)}\n =(-s_\\lambda)^{-k}\n   \\frac{\\prod_{v'\\in V}a(v')F_Y(v')}{\\prod_{p\\in H}A_Y(p)}\n   \\frac{\\det\\bigl((G_Y^{-1}m_{v'}^t)_p\\bigr)_{H,V}}\n        {\\det\\bigl(1/[v'-p]\\bigr)_{H,V}}.$$ If the positive weights <a href=\"#eq:Gaudin-weights\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:Gaudin-weights\">[eq:Gaudin-weights]</a> are defined, the last numerator equals $$\\label{eq:W-determinant}\n \\det\\bigl((G_Y^{-1}m_{v'}^t)_p\\bigr)_{H,V}\n =\\left(\\prod_{p\\in H}\\ell_p\\right)\n       \\det\\bigl(W_{v'}(p)\\bigr)_{H,V},\n \\qquad W_{v'}=(I+K\\ell)^{-1}m_{v'}^t.$$ Empty products and determinants have value one.",
      "explanation": "Scalar products and norms as determinant ratios with deleted columns.",

          "anchor": "eq:source-Gaudin-limit",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-hilbert-dual",
        "exact_quote": "For even $L$ and real inhomogeneities, $$\\label{eq:B-Hilbert-adjoint}\n \\mathsf B(x)^*=-\\mathsf C(\\bar x).$$ Thus for a distinct real on-shell list with $\\det G_Y\\ne0$, the physical unit vector $$\\label{eq:physical-Bethe-vector}\n \\Omega_Y=\\frac{\\mathcal P_L|Y\\rangle}{\\|\\mathcal P_L|Y\\rangle\\|}$$ is well defined, and $$\\|\\mathcal P_L|Y\\rangle\\|^2=(-1)^nS(Y,Y)>0.$$ If $Y,Z$ are two such real on-shell lists of the same size, possibly for different twists, then $$\\label{eq:two-orientation-overlap}\n |\\langle\\Omega_Y,\\Omega_Z\\rangle|^2\n       =\\frac{S(Y,Z)S(Z,Y)}{S(Y,Y)S(Z,Z)}.$$ These identities also hold for real staggered $b_j$, without a Perron interpretation of the vectors.",
        "source_id": "PM-225.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-hilbert-dual",
          "exact_quote": "For even $L$ and real inhomogeneities, $$\\label{eq:B-Hilbert-adjoint}\n \\mathsf B(x)^*=-\\mathsf C(\\bar x).$$ Thus for a distinct real on-shell list with $\\det G_Y\\ne0$, the physical unit vector $$\\label{eq:physical-Bethe-vector}\n \\Omega_Y=\\frac{\\mathcal P_L|Y\\rangle}{\\|\\mathcal P_L|Y\\rangle\\|}$$ is well defined, and $$\\|\\mathcal P_L|Y\\rangle\\|^2=(-1)^nS(Y,Y)>0.$$ If $Y,Z$ are two such real on-shell lists of the same size, possibly for different twists, then $$\\label{eq:two-orientation-overlap}\n |\\langle\\Omega_Y,\\Omega_Z\\rangle|^2\n       =\\frac{S(Y,Z)S(Z,Y)}{S(Y,Y)S(Z,Z)}.$$ These identities also hold for real staggered $b_j$, without a Perron interpretation of the vectors.",
          "source_id": "PM-225.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "For even $L$ and real inhomogeneities, $$\\label{eq:B-Hilbert-adjoint}\n \\mathsf B(x)^*=-\\mathsf C(\\bar x).$$ Thus for a distinct real on-shell list with $\\det G_Y\\ne0$, the physical unit vector $$\\label{eq:physical-Bethe-vector}\n \\Omega_Y=\\frac{\\mathcal P_L|Y\\rangle}{\\|\\mathcal P_L|Y\\rangle\\|}$$ is well defined, and $$\\|\\mathcal P_L|Y\\rangle\\|^2=(-1)^nS(Y,Y)>0.$$ If $Y,Z$ are two such real on-shell lists of the same size, possibly for different twists, then $$\\label{eq:two-orientation-overlap}\n |\\langle\\Omega_Y,\\Omega_Z\\rangle|^2\n       =\\frac{S(Y,Z)S(Z,Y)}{S(Y,Y)S(Z,Z)}.$$ These identities also hold for real staggered $b_j$, without a Perron interpretation of the vectors.",
      "explanation": "Creation entry adjoint is minus annihilation; Bethe vectors have positive norm.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",
      "hypotheses": [

          "anchor": "stmt-lem-hilbert-dual",
          "exact_quote": "For even $L$ and real inhomogeneities",
          "source_id": "PM-225.1"

      "kind": "theorem",
      "quantifiers": [],
      "statement_anchor": "stmt-lem-hilbert-dual"
    },
    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-cor-finite-word-expansion",
        "exact_quote": "Expand each auxiliary mark as $$T^{D_j}=\\sum_{\\alpha,\\beta}(D_j)_{\\beta\\alpha}\n M_{{\\rm ph},\\alpha\\beta}.$$ For each resulting entry word, let $n_j$ be the number of minus spins after its $j$th action, with $n_0=n$. Entry words with $n_m\\ne n$ have zero expectation in $\\Omega_Y$. For a path of a word with $n_m=n$, let $H\\subset Y$ be the roots absent at the end and let $V$ be its surviving added arguments, ordered in the same replacement slots. Then $|H|=|V|=:k\\le m$, and its contribution to the normalized physical expectation is $$\\label{eq:exact-path-determinant}\n \\left(\\prod_{j=1}^m(D_j)_{\\beta_j\\alpha_j}\\right)\n (-1)^{\\sum_{j=1}^m(n_{j-1}-n)}\n (-s_\\lambda)^{-k}\n \\frac{\\prod_{j=1}^m\\gamma_j}\n      {\\prod_{j=1}^m(1+q_Y(v'_j))}\n \\frac{\\det(W_{v'}(p))_{H,V}}\n      {\\det(1/[v'-p])_{H,V}}\n \\prod_{p\\in H}\\ell_p.$$ The expectation is the sum of these contributions. In particular, the determinant size is bounded by the word length. For diagonal entry words the parity sign in <a href=\"#eq:exact-path-determinant\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:exact-path-determinant\">[eq:exact-path-determinant]</a> is one. Among distinct removed original roots the correction product contains only reciprocal $f$ factors, with one for each pair. Each removed original root also contributes one factor $\\beta$ joining it to the row argument at the step that removes it.",
        "source_id": "PM-225.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

Entry words with $n_m\\ne n$ have zero expectation in $\\Omega_Y$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-root-limits",
        "exact_quote": "The zero-twist consecutive-root equations of Lemma <a href=\"#lem:bethe-data\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:bethe-data\">3</a> have distinct real solutions with the following properties.*\n\n1.  *If $\\lambda>\\pi/2$, the solutions at half filling exist for every finite homogeneous or real-array chain. Their counting derivative $\\rho_L=\\xi'$ is positive. If $$p_L(u)=\\sum_{j=0}^{L-1}r(u-b_j),$$ then $\\rho_L-p_L$ and their integrated discrepancy are uniformly bounded on the entire line.*\n\n\n\n2.  *In the homogeneous chain, let $$r_Q=(I+K_Q)^{-1}s,\\qquad H_Q=(I+K_Q)^{-1}1,$$ where $K_Q$ integrates over $[-Q,Q]$. For all $Q\\ge0$, $$\\label{eq:regular-density-comparison}\n     c r(u)\\le r_Q(u)\\le C r(u),\\qquad\n     c\\le H_Q(u)\\le C\\quad (|u|\\le Q).$$ The mass $\\int_{-Q}^Q r_Q$ increases to $1/2$, and $$\\frac12-\\int_{-Q}^Q r_Q\\asymp e^{-Q/\\eta}.$$ Choose $Q=Q_L$ by $\\int_{-Q}^Q r_Q=n/L$. If $Q$ stays bounded with a positive limiting filling, or if $Lr(Q)$ tends to infinity at least as a fixed positive power of $L$, the roots exist for all sufficiently large $L$. If $y_j^0$ are the mid-quantiles of $Lr_Q$, then $Lr_Q(y_j^0)|y_j-y_j^0|\\le C$ uniformly in $j$, and $$\\label{eq:regular-counting-density}\n     \\xi'(u)=Lr_Q(u)+O(1)$$ on the interval, including a bounded counting extension at its endpoints.*\n\n\n3.  *In all three limiting procedures the measures $$\\nu_L=\\sum_j\\ell_j\\delta_{y_j},\\qquad\n     \\ell_j=\\xi'(y_j)^{-1},$$ have uniformly bounded mass on intervals of length one. In a region whose slightly enlarged neighborhood has density at least $M$, their error against bounded Lipschitz tests on a fixed bounded interval is at most $$\\label{eq:local-quadrature}\n     \\frac{C}{M}\\bigl(\\|\\phi\\|_\\infty+\\mathop{\\mathrm{Lip}}(\\phi)\\bigr).$$ Thus $\\nu_L$ converges locally to Lebesgue measure in the dense portions of the limits.*\n\n*At zero twist the homogeneous vectors constructed from these roots are the sector Perron vectors. The Gaudin matrices are nonsingular, so each constructed finite root list has a real-analytic twist branch near zero.",
        "source_id": "PM-225.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

If $Q$ stays bounded with a positive limiting filling, or if $Lr(Q)$ tends to infinity at least as a fixed positive power of $L$, the roots exist for all sufficiently large $L$. If $y_j^0$ are the mid-quantiles of $Lr_Q$, then $Lr_Q(y_j^0)|y_j-y_j^0|\\le C$ uniformly in $j$, and $$\\label{eq:regular-counting-density}\n     \\xi'(u)=Lr_Q(u)+O(1)$$ on the interval, including a bounded counting extension at its endpoints.*\n\n\n3.  *In all three limiting procedures the measures $$\\nu_L=\\sum_j\\ell_j\\delta_{y_j},\\qquad\n     \\ell_j=\\xi'(y_j)^{-1},$$ have uniformly bounded mass on intervals of length one. In a region whose slightly enlarged neighborhood has density at least $M$, their error against bounded Lipschitz tests on a fixed bounded interval is at most $$\\label{eq:local-quadrature}\n     \\frac{C}{M}\\bigl(\\|\\phi\\|_\\infty+\\mathop{\\mathrm{Lip}}(\\phi)\\bigr).$$ Thus $\\nu_L$ converges locally to Lebesgue measure in the dense portions of the limits.*\n\n*At zero twist the homogeneous vectors constructed from these roots are the sector Perron vectors. The Gaudin matrices are nonsingular, so each constructed finite root list has a real-analytic twist branch near zero.",
      "explanation": "Construction of real roots, density comparison and quadrature estimate.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-gaudin-inverse",
        "exact_quote": "In the limits specified above, $$\\label{eq:Gaudin-sup-inverse}\n \\|(I+K\\ell)^{-1}\\|_{\\infty\\to\\infty}\\le C,\\qquad\n (K\\ell)_{ij}=K(y_i-y_j)\\ell_j.$$ There is also a small $a>0$ for which the inverse has uniformly bounded row norm after conjugation by either $e^{ay_i}$ or $e^{-ay_i}$.*\n\n*Let $\\chi_-,\\chi_0,\\chi_+$ be a nonnegative partition of unity whose two transitions are translates of fixed smooth profiles with bounded first derivatives. The middle function has compact support, the transitions lie in regions of a fixed sufficiently high density, and the two transition regions recede from one another. The left function is one to the left of its transition and zero to its right; the right function has the opposite convention at the right transition. Put $$D_L=\\nu_L-\\,\\mathrm du,\\qquad D^\\bullet=\\chi_\\bullet D_L.$$ The mixed operator $$\\label{eq:mixed-operator}\n I+R_0*(D^0+D^+)$$ has a uniform lower bound in the pointwise sup norm. The analogous assertion holds with the two ends interchanged.",
        "source_id": "PM-225.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-gaudin-inverse",
          "exact_quote": "In the limits specified above, $$\\label{eq:Gaudin-sup-inverse}\n \\|(I+K\\ell)^{-1}\\|_{\\infty\\to\\infty}\\le C,\\qquad\n (K\\ell)_{ij}=K(y_i-y_j)\\ell_j.$$ There is also a small $a>0$ for which the inverse has uniformly bounded row norm after conjugation by either $e^{ay_i}$ or $e^{-ay_i}$.*\n\n*Let $\\chi_-,\\chi_0,\\chi_+$ be a nonnegative partition of unity whose two transitions are translates of fixed smooth profiles with bounded first derivatives. The middle function has compact support, the transitions lie in regions of a fixed sufficiently high density, and the two transition regions recede from one another. The analogous assertion holds with the two ends interchanged.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-row-normalization",
        "exact_quote": "There is a real harmonic function $U$ on $\\mathcal S_\\lambda$ such that, for every finite real-inhomogeneity chain, $$\\label{eq:harmonic-row-bound}\n \\|T^D(x)\\|\\le2\\|D\\|\n                 \\exp\\left(\\sum_jU(x-b_j)\\right).$$ Choose a nonvanishing analytic function $\\mathcal N_L$ whose modulus is the exponential on the right. In the homogeneous plane and array limits, the untwisted vacuum eigenvalue $\\tau_L$ satisfies $$\\label{eq:vacuum-modulus-normalization}\n |\\tau_L(x)/\\mathcal N_L(x)|\\longrightarrow1$$ locally uniformly in the strip, with the moving-bulk convention <a href=\"#eq:array-limit-convention\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:array-limit-convention\">[eq:array-limit-convention]</a> in the array case. Consequently, for each compact $\\mathcal K\\subset\\mathcal S_\\lambda$, $$\\label{eq:normalized-row-bound}\n \\limsup\\sup_{x\\in\\mathcal K}\\|A_L^D(x)\\|\\le2\\|D\\|.$$ In particular the vacuum denominator has no zeros on such a compact for all sufficiently large systems in the indicated sense.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-weighted-flags",
        "exact_quote": "Let $E=\\mathbb C^m$ be the coefficient space of the fixed column list. For each admissible $\\beta>0$ there is a subspace $F_{\\beta,L}\\subset E$ of codimension at most $r_\\beta$ such that $$\\label{eq:weighted-node-decay}\n |W(c)(u)|\\le C_\\beta e^{-\\beta u}\\|c\\|_2\n \\quad\\hbox{at every positive root }u,\\quad c\\in F_{\\beta,L}.$$ For a fixed finite list of increasing shifts, choose one discrepancy partition for the whole list. The resulting subspaces may be chosen nested. There are analogous flags at the negative end. The constants are uniform in the three limiting procedures, with regular filling understood as $Q\\to\\infty$ after $L\\to\\infty$. Restriction to any column coordinate subspace preserves the codimension bound and all unit-vector estimates.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-joint-determinant-tails",
        "exact_quote": "For a fixed generic row word and any one of its nonzero finite action paths, let $H=(p_1,\\ldots,p_k)$ be the deleted original roots and $V$ its surviving row arguments. After extracting the quadrature weights $\\prod_{i=1}^k\\ell_{p_i}$ from <a href=\"#eq:exact-path-determinant\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:exact-path-determinant\">[eq:exact-path-determinant]</a>, the remaining summand is bounded in modulus by $$\\label{eq:joint-tail-majorant}\n C\\exp\\left(-\\varepsilon\\sum_{i=1}^k|p_i|\\right)$$ for some $\\varepsilon>0$. The constant and exponent may depend on the fixed word and generic compact parameter set, but are uniform in the limiting procedures. In particular, the sum over paths with $\\max_i|p_i|>R$ is $O(e^{-\\varepsilon'R})$ uniformly, for some $\\varepsilon'>0$.",
        "source_id": "PM-225.1"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-word-kernel",
        "exact_quote": "For each fixed integer $m\\ge0$ and fixed $2\\times2$ matrices $D_1,\\ldots,D_m$, the homogeneous plane coefficients $$\\label{eq:word-kernel-definition}\n \\left\\langle\\Omega_L,\n     A_L^{D_m}(x_m)\\cdots A_L^{D_1}(x_1)\\Omega_L\\right\\rangle$$ converge locally uniformly on $\\mathcal S_\\lambda^m$ to a holomorphic function $\\mathcal K_{D_m,\\ldots,D_1}(x_m,\\ldots,x_1)$. It is invariant under every common complex translation for which the translated arguments remain in the strip. For $\\lambda>2\\pi/3$, the same functions are the array limits in <a href=\"#eq:array-limit-convention\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:array-limit-convention\">[eq:array-limit-convention]</a>.*\n\n*If $v_L$ is any fixed finite linear combination of row words applied to $\\Omega_L$, then $$\\lim\\|A_L^D(x)v_L\\|^2\n       \\le4\\|D\\|^2\\lim\\|v_L\\|^2.$$ The two limits are determined by the word coefficients and the adjoint rule. This is the bound used to construct bounded row actions in Section <a href=\"#sec:angular\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"sec:angular\">4</a>. For $\\lambda>2\\pi/3$, in the regular-filling procedure the expectations of every fixed equal-time local matrix product converge to those of the homogeneous plane construction.",
        "source_id": "PM-225.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-word-kernel",
          "exact_quote": "For each fixed integer $m\\ge0$ and fixed $2\\times2$ matrices $D_1,\\ldots,D_m$, the homogeneous plane coefficients $$\\label{eq:word-kernel-definition}\n \\left\\langle\\Omega_L,\n     A_L^{D_m}(x_m)\\cdots A_L^{D_1}(x_1)\\Omega_L\\right\\rangle$$ converge locally uniformly on $\\mathcal S_\\lambda^m$ to a holomorphic function $\\mathcal K_{D_m,\\ldots,D_1}(x_m,\\ldots,x_1)$. It is invariant under every common complex translation for which the translated arguments remain in the strip. For $\\lambda>2\\pi/3$, the same functions are the array limits in <a href=\"#eq:array-limit-convention\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:array-limit-convention\">[eq:array-limit-convention]</a>.*\n\n*If $v_L$ is any fixed finite linear combination of row words applied to $\\Omega_L$, then $$\\lim\\|A_L^D(x)v_L\\|^2\n       \\le4\\|D\\|^2\\lim\\|v_L\\|^2.$$ The two limits are determined by the word coefficients and the adjoint rule. This is the bound used to construct bounded row actions in Section <a href=\"#sec:angular\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"sec:angular\">4</a>.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-word-clustering",
        "exact_quote": "Partition a fixed finite list of diagonal marked rows into groups. Within each group fix its internal arguments in the strict angle strip. Translate the arguments of group $g$ by a common imaginary amount $it_g$, and let every $|t_g-t_{g'}|$ tend to infinity. Then the plane word kernel factors into the product of the individual group kernels. Convergence is locally uniform in the internal strict arguments. The order within each group is retained.",
        "source_id": "PM-225.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-word-clustering",
          "exact_quote": "Partition a fixed finite list of diagonal marked rows into groups. Within each group fix its internal arguments in the strict angle strip. Then the plane word kernel factors into the product of the individual group kernels. Convergence is locally uniform in the internal strict arguments. The order within each group is retained.",
          "source_id": "PM-225.1"
        }
      ],
```
