      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-angular-space",
        "exact_quote": "On $\\mathcal H$ the rows act as bounded holomorphic operator families, with $$\\|A^D(z)\\|\\le 2\\|D\\|,\\qquad\n A^D(z)^*=A^{X\\overline D X}(-\\overline z).$$ They satisfy the finite RTT exchange identities wherever their auxiliary intertwiner is invertible. There is a strongly continuous unitary group $U_b$, $b\\in\\mathbb R$, fixing $\\Omega$, such that $$\\label{eq:angular-covariance}\n U_bA^D(z)U_b^*=A^D(z+ib).$$ Words with real arguments in any nonempty open subinterval of $(-v,v)$ span a dense subspace. The family $P=A^I$ consists of commuting normal operators and has a simultaneous representation by almost surely holomorphic scalar functions $f$ on the strip, with $$\\label{eq:angular-scalar-star}\n f(-\\overline z)=\\overline{f(z)},\\qquad |f(z)|\\le2.$$ In the half-plane coordinate this reality condition reads $f(\\overline w)=\\overline{f(w)}$.",
        "source_id": "PM-225.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-angular-space",
          "exact_quote": "On $\\mathcal H$ the rows act as bounded holomorphic operator families, with $$\\|A^D(z)\\|\\le 2\\|D\\|,\\qquad\n A^D(z)^*=A^{X\\overline D X}(-\\overline z).$$ They satisfy the finite RTT exchange identities wherever their auxiliary intertwiner is invertible. There is a strongly continuous unitary group $U_b$, $b\\in\\mathbb R$, fixing $\\Omega$, such that $$\\label{eq:angular-covariance}\n U_bA^D(z)U_b^*=A^D(z+ib).$$ Words with real arguments in any nonempty open subinterval of $(-v,v)$ span a dense subspace. The family $P=A^I$ consists of commuting normal operators and has a simultaneous representation by almost surely holomorphic scalar functions $f$ on the strip, with $$\\label{eq:angular-scalar-star}\n f(-\\overline z)=\\overline{f(z)},\\qquad |f(z)|\\le2.$$ In the half-plane coordinate this reality condition reads $f(\\overline w)=\\overline{f(w)}$.",
          "source_id": "PM-225.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "On $\\mathcal H$ the rows act as bounded holomorphic operator families, with $$\\|A^D(z)\\|\\le 2\\|D\\|,\\qquad\n A^D(z)^*=A^{X\\overline D X}(-\\overline z).$$ They satisfy the finite RTT exchange identities wherever their auxiliary intertwiner is invertible. There is a strongly continuous unitary group $U_b$, $b\\in\\mathbb R$, fixing $\\Omega$, such that $$\\label{eq:angular-covariance}\n U_bA^D(z)U_b^*=A^D(z+ib).$$ Words with real arguments in any nonempty open subinterval of $(-v,v)$ span a dense subspace. The family $P=A^I$ consists of commuting normal operators and has a simultaneous representation by almost surely holomorphic scalar functions $f$ on the strip, with $$\\label{eq:angular-scalar-star}\n f(-\\overline z)=\\overline{f(z)},\\qquad |f(z)|\\le2.$$ In the half-plane coordinate this reality condition reads $f(\\overline w)=\\overline{f(w)}$.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-scalar-continuation",
        "exact_quote": "There is $r_\\lambda>0$, independent of the even chain length, for which the two homogeneous quotients $$T^D(z)T^I(z)^{-1},\\qquad T^I(z)^{-1}T^D(z)$$ are holomorphic and bounded by $C_\\lambda\\|D\\|$ on discs of radius $r_\\lambda$ about $z=v$ and $z=-v$. The limiting rows have strong boundary values $$\\label{eq:angular-endpoints}\n A^D(v)=SD_0,\\qquad\n A^D(-v)=(XD^tX)_0S^{-1},$$ where $S$ is the unitary site shift and $S\\Omega=\\Omega$. Almost every spectral function extends analytically and without zeros to bands of a common positive width about $\\operatorname{Re}z=\\pm v$, has modulus one on these lines, and satisfies $|f|\\le1$ between them. Consequently it is inner in the right half-plane, and every zero $\\zeta$ satisfies $$\\label{eq:angular-cone}\n \\operatorname{Re}\\zeta\\ge c_0|\\zeta|$$ for a fixed $c_0>0$. At each specified strict argument the spectral set on which $f$ vanishes has measure zero.",
        "source_id": "PM-225.1"
      },

        {

The limiting rows have strong boundary values $$\\label{eq:angular-endpoints}\n A^D(v)=SD_0,\\qquad\n A^D(-v)=(XD^tX)_0S^{-1},$$ where $S$ is the unitary site shift and $S\\Omega=\\Omega$.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-physical-plane",
        "exact_quote": "The row representation gives the local probabilities of the plane law $\\mathbb P_c$ in Theorem <a href=\"#thm:main\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:main\">1</a>. In particular its prescribed iterated balanced-torus limit exists when $c<1$. Quantization with time pointing east and quantization with time pointing north describe this same law. For an ordinary observable supported on one side of either coordinate cut, the squared norm of its prepared state is its expectation paired with its complex-conjugate reflected observable, with the corresponding spin reflection. Products of neutral height observables may be prepared using paths confined to that side.",
        "source_id": "PM-225.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-physical-plane",
          "exact_quote": "The row representation gives the local probabilities of the plane law $\\mathbb P_c$ in Theorem <a href=\"#thm:main\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:main\">1</a>. In particular its prescribed iterated balanced-torus limit exists when $c<1$. Quantization with time pointing east and quantization with time pointing north describe this same law. For an ordinary observable supported on one side of either coordinate cut, the squared norm of its prepared state is its expectation paired with its complex-conjugate reflected observable, with the corresponding spin reflection. Products of neutral height observables may be prepared using paths confined to that side.",
          "source_id": "PM-225.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "The row representation gives the local probabilities of the plane law $\\mathbb P_c$ in Theorem <a href=\"#thm:main\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:main\">1</a>.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-inner-spectra",
        "exact_quote": "On $\\mathcal D$, almost every spectral function is either $1$ or a finite Blaschke product in the right half-plane, with $$\\label{eq:finite-inner-symmetry}\n f(0)=f(\\infty)=1,\\qquad\n f(\\overline w)=\\overline{f(w)}.$$ Its zeros obey <a href=\"#eq:angular-cone\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:angular-cone\">[eq:angular-cone]</a>. In particular its degree is even; write it as $2n_f$ when it is nonconstant. Every zero produces a fixed-width, fixed-depth dip of $|f(e^s)|$ near its logarithmic radius.*\n\n\n\n\n\n*There is a possibly infinite positive measure $\\mu$ on the nonconstant functions and a measurable spectral vector $\\mathsf h$ such that $$\\label{eq:current-cocycle}\n J(w)\\Omega=(1-f(w))\\mathsf h,\n \\qquad \\mu(\\,\\mathrm df)=\\|\\mathsf h(f)\\|^2\\,\\sigma(\\,\\mathrm df).$$ Here $\\sigma$ is any scalar measure used for the direct-integral representation; the resulting measure $\\mu$ does not depend on that choice. It is invariant under dilation of the argument. On the cross-section where the smallest zero radius is one, it has the form $$\\label{eq:orbit-disintegration}\n \\mu=\\,\\mathrm ds\\,\\nu(\\,\\mathrm dF),\\qquad f(w)=F(e^s w),\\qquad\n \\kappa:=\\nu(1)<\\infty.$$ If $\\mathcal L(F)$ is the difference between its largest and smallest logarithmic zero radii, then $$\\label{eq:orbit-width-tail}\n \\nu\\{\\mathcal L>t\\}\\le Ce^{-2\\eta t}\\quad(t\\ge1).$$ For a fixed diagonal word $h$, $$\\label{eq:diagonal-boost-tail}\n \\|(1-P(e^s))h\\|\\le C_h e^{-\\eta|s|}.$$ For products of fixed words placed at scales $r_i$, the corresponding bound outside their fixed internal argument ranges is $C\\sum_i e^{-\\eta|s-\\log r_i|}$, uniformly when these ranges separate.",
        "source_id": "PM-225.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-inner-spectra",

In particular its degree is even; write it as $2n_f$ when it is nonconstant. Every zero produces a fixed-width, fixed-depth dip of $|f(e^s)|$ near its logarithmic radius.*\n\n\n\n\n\n*There is a possibly infinite positive measure $\\mu$ on the nonconstant functions and a measurable spectral vector $\\mathsf h$ such that $$\\label{eq:current-cocycle}\n J(w)\\Omega=(1-f(w))\\mathsf h,\n \\qquad \\mu(\\,\\mathrm df)=\\|\\mathsf h(f)\\|^2\\,\\sigma(\\,\\mathrm df).$$ Here $\\sigma$ is any scalar measure used for the direct-integral representation; the resulting measure $\\mu$ does not depend on that choice.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-low-band",
        "exact_quote": "There are constants $A,a,C,u_0>0$ such that, for $0<u<u_0$, $Q_uJ(w)Q_u$ extends to a bounded holomorphic function on the full annulus $Au<|w|<a/u$. If $$\\label{eq:angular-Laurent}\n Q_uJ(w)Q_u=\\sum_{j\\in\\mathbb Z}w^j J_{u,j},$$ then $\\|J_{u,j}\\|\\le C(Cu)^{|j|}$, with the exponent zero for $j=0$. Set $\\theta=\\eta/2>0$. Uniformly on the closed right unit semicircle, $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\|Q_u^\\sigma J(w)Q_u^\\sigma\\|&\\le Cu,\n &\\|Q_u^\\sigma J(w)Q_u^{-\\sigma}\\|&\\le Cu^\\theta,\n \\label{eq:angular-J-bands}\\\\\n Q_u^\\sigma N(w)Q_u^\\sigma\n &=\\sigma Q_u^\\sigma(wJ_{u,1}+w^{-1}J_{u,-1})Q_u^\\sigma+O(u^2),\n \\label{eq:angular-N-leading}\\\\\n \\|Q_u^\\sigma N(w)Q_u^{-\\sigma}\\|&\\le Cu^{1+\\theta},\n &\\|Q_uN(w)Q_u\\|&\\le Cu.\n \\label{eq:angular-N-bands}\n\\end{aligned}$$ The estimates remain valid for unequal input and output cutoffs, using their maximum for $u$. With $m$ ordinary time rows on each side, $N$ costs $O(m^{-1})$ and a sign-opposite undoubled $J$ costs $O(m^{-\\theta})$. The leading-mode remainder is $O(m^{-1-\\theta})$ when, on each dyadic input/output block, the leading expression is taken at the larger cutoff; the precise block sum is defined in the proof.*\n\n\n*Finally, if $S=P(i)$ and $K$ is the principal argument of $S^2$, then $$\\label{eq:angular-phase-bound}\n S^2=e^{iK},\\qquad |K|\\le C H_0.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-225.1"
      },

With $m$ ordinary time rows on each side, $N$ costs $O(m^{-1})$ and a sign-opposite undoubled $J$ costs $O(m^{-\\theta})$. The leading-mode remainder is $O(m^{-1-\\theta})$ when, on each dyadic input/output block, the leading expression is taken at the larger cutoff; the precise block sum is defined in the proof.*\n\n\n*Finally, if $S=P(i)$ and $K$ is the principal argument of $S^2$, then $$\\label{eq:angular-phase-bound}\n S^2=e^{iK},\\qquad |K|\\le C H_0.$$",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-endpoint-hamiltonian",
        "exact_quote": "The nonnegative self-adjoint spectral operator $$\\label{eq:angular-endpoint-energy}\n E=S^{-1}\\partial_zP(z)\\big|_{z=v},\\qquad\n E(f)=-\\left.\\partial_s\\arg f(ie^s)\\right|_{s=0},$$ agrees on local-spin vectors with the vacuum-subtracted nearest-neighbor Hamiltonian whose link is $$\\label{eq:angular-local-link}\n h_{j,j+1}=-\\eta\\,\\operatorname{swap}_{j,j+1}R'_{j,j+1}(0)\n =\\begin{pmatrix}\n \\eta\\cot\\lambda&0&0&0\\\\\n 0&0&-\\eta/\\sin\\lambda&0\\\\\n 0&-\\eta/\\sin\\lambda&0&0\\\\\n 0&0&0&\\eta\\cot\\lambda\n \\end{pmatrix}_{j,j+1}.$$ In particular $[h_{j,j+1},Z_j+Z_{j+1}]=0$ and $E\\Omega=0$. The closure of the local-spin subspace reduces $E$, and its real-time evolution is the local Hamiltonian evolution. For all sufficiently small $u$, $$\\label{eq:angular-E-H-comparison}\n \\mathbf 1_{\\{E\\le u\\}}\\le\\mathbf 1_{\\{H_0\\le Cu\\}}.$$ With $Z_1=S^{-1}Z_0S$, the exact adjacent-pair identity is $$\\label{eq:angular-adjacent-current}\n N(i)=\\tfrac12 S^2(Z_0+Z_1).$$",
        "source_id": "PM-225.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-endpoint-hamiltonian",
          "exact_quote": "The nonnegative self-adjoint spectral operator $$\\label{eq:angular-endpoint-energy}\n E=S^{-1}\\partial_zP(z)\\big|_{z=v},\\qquad\n E(f)=-\\left.\\partial_s\\arg f(ie^s)\\right|_{s=0},$$ agrees on local-spin vectors with the vacuum-subtracted nearest-neighbor Hamiltonian whose link is $$\\label{eq:angular-local-link}\n h_{j,j+1}=-\\eta\\,\\operatorname{swap}_{j,j+1}R'_{j,j+1}(0)\n =\\begin{pmatrix}\n \\eta\\cot\\lambda&0&0&0\\\\\n 0&0&-\\eta/\\sin\\lambda&0\\\\\n 0&-\\eta/\\sin\\lambda&0&0\\\\\n 0&0&0&\\eta\\cot\\lambda\n \\end{pmatrix}_{j,j+1}.$$ In particular $[h_{j,j+1},Z_j+Z_{j+1}]=0$ and $E\\Omega=0$. The closure of the local-spin subspace reduces $E$, and its real-time evolution is the local Hamiltonian evolution. For all sufficiently small $u$, $$\\label{eq:angular-E-H-comparison}\n \\mathbf 1_{\\{E\\le u\\}}\\le\\mathbf 1_{\\{H_0\\le Cu\\}}.$$ With $Z_1=S^{-1}Z_0S$, the exact adjacent-pair identity is $$\\label{eq:angular-adjacent-current}\n N(i)=\\tfrac12 S^2(Z_0+Z_1).$$",

      "exact_statement": "The nonnegative self-adjoint spectral operator $$\\label{eq:angular-endpoint-energy}\n E=S^{-1}\\partial_zP(z)\\big|_{z=v},\\qquad\n E(f)=-\\left.\\partial_s\\arg f(ie^s)\\right|_{s=0},$$ agrees on local-spin vectors with the vacuum-subtracted nearest-neighbor Hamiltonian whose link is $$\\label{eq:angular-local-link}\n h_{j,j+1}=-\\eta\\,\\operatorname{swap}_{j,j+1}R'_{j,j+1}(0)\n =\\begin{pmatrix}\n \\eta\\cot\\lambda&0&0&0\\\\\n 0&0&-\\eta/\\sin\\lambda&0\\\\\n 0&-\\eta/\\sin\\lambda&0&0\\\\\n 0&0&0&\\eta\\cot\\lambda\n \\end{pmatrix}_{j,j+1}.$$ In particular $[h_{j,j+1},Z_j+Z_{j+1}]=0$ and $E\\Omega=0$.         "anchor": "stmt-lem-sum-rule",
        "exact_quote": "Let $p$ denote the phase of the unit shift $S=P(i)$, modulo $2\\pi$, and let $\\operatorname{sym}$ average a measure at $p$ and $-p$. Set $$D_0=-\\langle\\Omega,[Z_0,[Z_0,h_{0,1}]]\\Omega\\rangle.$$ Away from $p=0$, the projection of the current measure obeys $$\\label{eq:angular-sum-rule}\n 2\\operatorname{sym}\\bigl((p)_*(E\\mu)\\bigr)\n       =D_0\\frac{\\,\\mathrm dp}{2\\pi}.$$ On each orbit of degree $2n_F$, the phase winds monotonically through $n_F$ complete turns. In particular $$\\label{eq:angular-degree-integral}\n \\int n_F\\,\\mathrm d\\nu(F)<\\infty.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-225.1"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-prop-variogram",
        "exact_quote": "After an axial symmetry, any nonzero lattice displacement may be written $(k,j)$ with $k\\ge1$ and $|j|\\le k$. Its height variance is $$\\label{eq:angular-variogram}\n \\begin{aligned}\n \\mathbb E_c\\bigl(h(k,j)-h(0,0)\\bigr)^2\n &=2\\operatorname{Re}\\int\\bigl(1-f(1)^k f(\\pm i)^{|j|}\\bigr)\\,\\mathrm d\\mu(f)\\\\\n &=4\\kappa\\log k+O(1),\n \\end{aligned}$$ where the sign of $i$ is chosen according to the vertical direction. The error is uniform over these displacements. In particular the variance is bounded by $C(1+\\log(1+|k|+|j|))$.",

      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-normalization",
        "exact_quote": "With the row and height conventions of Sections <a href=\"#sec:finite\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"sec:finite\">2</a> and <a href=\"#sec:angular\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"sec:angular\">4</a>, $$\\label{eq:kappa-value}\n \\kappa=\\frac1{2\\pi(\\pi-\\lambda)}.$$ Consequently a limiting covariance $-2\\kappa\\log|x-y|$ has squared multiplier $4\\pi\\kappa=2/(\\pi-\\lambda)=\\sigma(c)^2$ relative to <a href=\"#eq:green\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:green\">[eq:green]</a>.",
        "source_id": "PM-225.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-normalization",
          "exact_quote": "With the row and height conventions of Sections <a href=\"#sec:finite\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"sec:finite\">2</a> and <a href=\"#sec:angular\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"sec:angular\">4</a>, $$\\label{eq:kappa-value}\n \\kappa=\\frac1{2\\pi(\\pi-\\lambda)}.$$ Consequently a limiting covariance $-2\\kappa\\log|x-y|$ has squared multiplier $4\\pi\\kappa=2/(\\pi-\\lambda)=\\sigma(c)^2$ relative to <a href=\"#eq:green\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:green\">[eq:green]</a>.",

        }

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-chiral-forms",
        "exact_quote": "There are consistent skew-adjoint forms $j_\\pm$ on $\\mathcal D_\\pm^{\\mathrm{fin}}$ such that, for every $R>0$, $$\\label{eq:chiral-form-bound}\n \\|E_\\pm(R)j_\\pm E_\\pm(R)\\|\\le CR,\n \\qquad E_\\pm(R)j_\\pm\\Omega=E_\\pm(R)a_\\pm\\mathsf h.$$ Their vacuum means vanish. For $s>0$, the damped forms extend to bounded operators and satisfy $$\\label{eq:chiral-damped-form}\n                     \\|e^{-sa_\\pm}j_\\pm e^{-sa_\\pm}\\|\\le C/s.$$ For every nonnegative measurable function $g$ on $(0,\\infty)$, $$\\label{eq:chiral-Haar-integral}\n             \\int g(a_\\pm(f))\\,\\mathrm d\\mu(f)\n                     =\\kappa\\int_0^\\infty g(t)\\frac{dt}{t}.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-225.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-chiral-forms",
          "exact_quote": "There are consistent skew-adjoint forms $j_\\pm$ on $\\mathcal D_\\pm^{\\mathrm{fin}}$ such that, for every $R>0$, $$\\label{eq:chiral-form-bound}\n \\|E_\\pm(R)j_\\pm E_\\pm(R)\\|\\le CR,\n \\qquad E_\\pm(R)j_\\pm\\Omega=E_\\pm(R)a_\\pm\\mathsf h.$$ Their vacuum means vanish. For $s>0$, the damped forms extend to bounded operators and satisfy $$\\label{eq:chiral-damped-form}\n                     \\|e^{-sa_\\pm}j_\\pm e^{-sa_\\pm}\\|\\le C/s.$$ For every nonnegative measurable function $g$ on $(0,\\infty)$, $$\\label{eq:chiral-Haar-integral}\n             \\int g(a_\\pm(f))\\,\\mathrm d\\mu(f)\n                     =\\kappa\\int_0^\\infty g(t)\\frac{dt}{t}.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-225.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "There are consistent skew-adjoint forms $j_\\pm$ on $\\mathcal D_\\pm^{\\mathrm{fin}}$ such that, for every $R>0$, $$\\label{eq:chiral-form-bound}\n \\|E_\\pm(R)j_\\pm E_\\pm(R)\\|\\le CR,\n \\qquad E_\\pm(R)j_\\pm\\Omega=E_\\pm(R)a_\\pm\\mathsf h.$$ Their vacuum means vanish.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-chiral-representatives",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose $T_\\delta$ are uniformly bounded operators and, on every finite word tensor, $T_\\delta u_\\delta$ converges strongly to $Tu$. Then $T$ extends to a bounded operator on the tensor completion, and $T_\\delta v_\\delta\\to Tv$ for every strongly converging family $v_\\delta\\to v$. Such operator limits may be composed, without a commutation assumption.*\n\n*A family $v_\\delta$ converges strongly to $v$ if its pairings with all core representatives converge to the pairings with $v$ and its norms converge to $\\|v\\|$.",
        "source_id": "PM-225.1"
      },

Such operator limits may be composed, without a commutation assumption.*\n\n*A family $v_\\delta$ converges strongly to $v$ if its pairings with all core representatives converge to the pairings with $v$ and its norms converge to $\\|v\\|$.",
          "source_id": "PM-225.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Suppose $T_\\delta$ are uniformly bounded operators and, on every finite word tensor, $T_\\delta u_\\delta$ converges strongly to $Tu$. Such operator limits may be composed, without a commutation assumption.*\n\n*A family $v_\\delta$ converges strongly to $v$ if its pairings with all core representatives converge to the pairings with $v$ and its norms converge to $\\|v\\|$.",

        "anchor": "stmt-prop-two-scale",
        "exact_quote": "Take $r_1=\\delta^{-1}$ and $r_2=\\delta$, with $\\delta\\downarrow0$ through arbitrary positive real values. On core representatives, the joint spectral functions $(f(\\delta^{-1}\\,\\cdot),f(\\delta\\,\\cdot))$ converge in law in the compact-open topology of the right half-plane to the two tensor-factor functions $(F_1,F_2)$. Jointly with these functions, $$\\label{eq:chiral-scaled-spectrum}\n\\begin{split}\n \\delta^{-1}H_0&\\longrightarrow A:=a_+\\otimes I+I\\otimes a_-,\\\\\n \\delta^{-1}\\arg S&\\longrightarrow -a_+\\otimes I+I\\otimes a_-,\n       \\qquad \\mathop{\\mathrm{sgn}}P(1)\\longrightarrow I\n\\end{split}$$ in spectral law. The argument is the principal argument near zero. There is an unbounded set of $R>0$ for which $$\\label{eq:chiral-band-limit}\n Q_\\delta(R):=\\mathbf 1_{H_0/\\delta\\le R}\n                       \\longrightarrow Q(R):=\\mathbf 1_{A\\le R}$$ strongly on representatives. Bounded spectral multipliers obtained jointly from these limits have the same strong interpretation whenever their discontinuity sets have zero limiting state mass.*\n\n\n*If $m_\\delta\\ge0$ and $l_\\delta$ are integers with $\\delta m_\\delta\\to x\\ge0$ and $\\delta l_\\delta\\to y$, then $$\\label{eq:chiral-semigroup-limit}\n P(1)^{m_\\delta}S^{l_\\delta}\\longrightarrow\n       e^{-(x+iy)a_+}\\otimes e^{-(x-iy)a_-}$$ strongly on representatives.",
        "source_id": "PM-225.1"
      },
      "evidence": [

          "anchor": "stmt-prop-two-scale",

Jointly with these functions, $$\\label{eq:chiral-scaled-spectrum}\n\\begin{split}\n \\delta^{-1}H_0&\\longrightarrow A:=a_+\\otimes I+I\\otimes a_-,\\\\\n \\delta^{-1}\\arg S&\\longrightarrow -a_+\\otimes I+I\\otimes a_-,\n       \\qquad \\mathop{\\mathrm{sgn}}P(1)\\longrightarrow I\n\\end{split}$$ in spectral law. Bounded spectral multipliers obtained jointly from these limits have the same strong interpretation whenever their discontinuity sets have zero limiting state mass.*\n\n\n*If $m_\\delta\\ge0$ and $l_\\delta$ are integers with $\\delta m_\\delta\\to x\\ge0$ and $\\delta l_\\delta\\to y$, then $$\\label{eq:chiral-semigroup-limit}\n P(1)^{m_\\delta}S^{l_\\delta}\\longrightarrow\n       e^{-(x+iy)a_+}\\otimes e^{-(x-iy)a_-}$$ strongly on representatives.",

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-current-limit",
        "exact_quote": "For every admissible $R>0$ in Proposition <a href=\"#prop:two-scale\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:two-scale\">30</a> and every $|w|=1$ with $\\operatorname{Re}w\\ge0$, $$\\label{eq:chiral-current-limit}\n \\delta^{-1}Q_\\delta(R)N(w)Q_\\delta(R)\n \\longrightarrow\n Q(R)\\bigl(w(j_+\\otimes I)+w^{-1}(I\\otimes j_-)\\bigr)Q(R)$$ strongly on representatives.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-chiral-holomorphy",
        "exact_quote": "Let $$U_x=\\{(z_1,\\ldots,z_k):\\operatorname{Re}z_i\\ne\\operatorname{Re}z_j\\text{ for }i\\ne j\\},\n \\quad\n U_y=\\{(z_1,\\ldots,z_k):\\operatorname{Im}z_i\\ne\\operatorname{Im}z_j\\text{ for }i\\ne j\\}.$$ The function $F_k$ has a symmetric, jointly holomorphic extension to $U_x\\cup U_y$, and is the limit of the centered $+$ derivative correlations on both sets.*\n\n\n*Fix $z_2,\\ldots,z_k$ with distinct real coordinates and distinct imaginary coordinates. As a function of $z_1$, the extension is holomorphic away from the grid points $$\\label{eq:chiral-grid-points}\n                    p_{jh}=\\operatorname{Re}z_j+i\\operatorname{Im}z_h,\\qquad 2\\le j,h\\le k.$$ Near any such point it is $O(|z_1-p_{jh}|^{-2})$, locally uniformly as the fixed points vary while retaining these coordinate separations. For $k\\ge2$ it tends to zero as $|z_1|\\to\\infty$ with the other points fixed.",
        "source_id": "PM-225.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-chiral-holomorphy",
          "exact_quote": "Let $$U_x=\\{(z_1,\\ldots,z_k):\\operatorname{Re}z_i\\ne\\operatorname{Re}z_j\\text{ for }i\\ne j\\},\n \\quad\n U_y=\\{(z_1,\\ldots,z_k):\\operatorname{Im}z_i\\ne\\operatorname{Im}z_j\\text{ for }i\\ne j\\}.$$ The function $F_k$ has a symmetric, jointly holomorphic extension to $U_x\\cup U_y$, and is the limit of the centered $+$ derivative correlations on both sets.*\n\n\n*Fix $z_2,\\ldots,z_k$ with distinct real coordinates and distinct imaginary coordinates. As a function of $z_1$, the extension is holomorphic away from the grid points $$\\label{eq:chiral-grid-points}\n                    p_{jh}=\\operatorname{Re}z_j+i\\operatorname{Im}z_h,\\qquad 2\\le j,h\\le k.$$ Near any such point it is $O(|z_1-p_{jh}|^{-2})$, locally uniformly as the fixed points vary while retaining these coordinate separations. For $k\\ge2$ it tends to zero as $|z_1|\\to\\infty$ with the other points fixed.",
          "source_id": "PM-225.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $$U_x=\\{(z_1,\\ldots,z_k):\\operatorname{Re}z_i\\ne\\operatorname{Re}z_j\\text{ for }i\\ne j\\},\n \\quad\n U_y=\\{(z_1,\\ldots,z_k):\\operatorname{Im}z_i\\ne\\operatorname{Im}z_j\\text{ for }i\\ne j\\}.$$ The function $F_k$ has a symmetric, jointly holomorphic extension to $U_x\\cup U_y$, and is the limit of the centered $+$ derivative correlations on both sets.*\n\n\n*Fix $z_2,\\ldots,z_k$ with distinct real coordinates and distinct imaginary coordinates. As a function of $z_1$, the extension is holomorphic away from the grid points $$\\label{eq:chiral-grid-points}\n                    p_{jh}=\\operatorname{Re}z_j+i\\operatorname{Im}z_h,\\qquad 2\\le j,h\\le k.$$ Near any such point it is $O(|z_1-p_{jh}|^{-2})$, locally uniformly as the fixed points vary while retaining these coordinate separations.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-collision",
        "exact_quote": "For $a=a_+$ and $j=j_+$, the bounded operators $$\\label{eq:chiral-collision-operator}\n K_\\epsilon=e^{-\\epsilon a}(\\epsilon j)e^{-\\epsilon a}\n                      (\\epsilon j)e^{-\\epsilon a}$$ are uniformly bounded for $\\epsilon>0$ and satisfy $$\\label{eq:chiral-collision-limit}\n                    K_\\epsilon\\rightharpoonup-\\kappa I\n                       \\qquad(\\epsilon\\downarrow0).$$ Consequently, fix $z_2,\\ldots,z_k$ with pairwise distinct real coordinates and pairwise distinct imaginary coordinates, and let $2\\le j\\le k$. Then $$\\label{eq:chiral-double-pole}\n \\lim_{\\epsilon\\downarrow0}\\epsilon^2\n F_k(z_j+\\epsilon,z_2,\\ldots,z_k)\n   =-\\kappa F_{k-2}(z_2,\\ldots,\\widehat z_j,\\ldots,z_k).$$ The coefficient on the right is independent of both colliding positions.",
        "source_id": "PM-225.1"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-prop-gaussian-currents",
        "exact_quote": "Let $k\\ge1$, and let $z_1,\\ldots,z_k$ have pairwise distinct real coordinates or pairwise distinct imaginary coordinates. For $\\varepsilon_i\\in\\{+,-\\}$ and moving mesh faces $z_{i,\\delta}\\to z_i$, $$\\label{eq:chiral-Gaussian-current-limit}\n \\lim_{\\delta\\downarrow0}\n \\mathbb E\\prod_{i=1}^k\n       \\delta^{-1}D_\\delta^{\\varepsilon_i}h_\\delta(z_{i,\\delta})\n   =\\sum_{\\pi}\\ \\prod_{\\{i,j\\}\\in\\pi}\n                  C_{\\varepsilon_i,\\varepsilon_j}(z_i,z_j),$$ where the sum is over pairings, is zero for odd $k$, and $$\\label{eq:chiral-contractions}\n C_{++}(z,\\xi)=-\\frac{\\kappa}{(z-\\xi)^2},\\qquad\n C_{--}(z,\\xi)=-\\frac{\\kappa}{(\\bar z-\\bar\\xi)^2},\\qquad\n C_{+-}(z,\\xi)=C_{-+}(z,\\xi)=0.$$ Convergence is uniform on compact subsets of either separated-time region. By linearity the corresponding assertion holds for all products of the real centered derivatives $\\delta^{-1}D_{x,\\delta}$ and $\\delta^{-1}D_{y,\\delta}$. These are the off-diagonal derivatives of the Gaussian covariance $$\\label{eq:chiral-log-covariance}\n                              K_\\kappa(z,\\xi)=-2\\kappa\\log|z-\\xi|.$$",

      },

        "exact_quote": "Fix $0<A<\\infty$.         "exact_quote": "Fix an integer $k\\ge2$, constants $A,c_0,c_1>0$, a separation $d_0>0$, and a bounded region containing all factor centers. Choose $r_0=a_kd_0$, with $a_k>0$ sufficiently small in terms of these fixed data. Let $n\\le k$ and let $$A_i=\\langle h_\\delta,\\mu_i\\rangle,\\qquad 1\\le i\\le n,$$ where each $\\mu_i$ is a finite signed measure of mass zero, total variation at most $A$, and support in a square of side $Ar_i$ about $b_i$. Assume $\\|A_i\\|_{L^{2k}}\\le A$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-negative-regularity",
        "exact_quote": "For every bounded open $U\\subset\\mathbb R^2$ and every $-1<\\alpha<0$, the pinned fields converge in law, with tightness, in the ordinary restriction spaces $$\\mathcal C^\\alpha_{\\mathrm{res}}(U):=B^\\alpha_{\\infty,\\infty}(U),\\qquad\n B^\\alpha_{p,q}(U),\\qquad W^{\\alpha,p}(U),\n \\quad 1\\le p<\\infty,\\quad1\\le q\\le\\infty,$$ to the corresponding pinning of $\\mathcal H_\\kappa$. Here the Hölder laws are supported on the separable closure of smooth functions in the displayed norm. Convergence also holds in the literal outward-scaling completion defined by (16) of (Duminil-Copin, Kozlowski, Lammers, et al. 2026, Definition 2.6(iii)) [[DKLM2026](#ref-DKLM2026)]. No boundary regularity of $U$ is assumed. An intrinsic dual interpretation of the negative Sobolev norms is treated at the end of this section.",
        "source_id": "PM-225.1"
      },
      "evidence": [

          "anchor": "stmt-prop-negative-regularity",

        "anchor": "stmt-prop-intrinsic-dual",
        "exact_quote": "Let $U\\subset\\mathbb R^2$ be bounded and open, let $0<s<1$, and let $1\\le p<\\infty$, with conjugate exponent $q=p'$. The pinned fields converge in law, with norm tightness, in $(W^{s,q}(U))^*$ to the canonically pinned Gaussian field with kernel $K_\\kappa$. The laws are supported on a separable closed subspace of this dual. No extension property or regularity of $\\partial U$ is required.",
        "source_id": "PM-225.1"
      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-auto-remark-1",
        "exact_quote": "If a negative Besov convention is dual to a positive intrinsic Besov difference norm, choose $0<t<s<1$. The positive test norm of order $s$ controls the intrinsic $W^{t,q}$ norm: in a dyadic difference expansion, the gain $2^{-j(s-t)}$ is summable, for every sequence exponent including its endpoints. The convergence just proved for $(W^{t,q}(U))^*$ therefore gives the corresponding negative Besov dual convergence of order $-s$. This is in addition to the ordinary restriction Besov conclusion of Proposition <a href=\"#prop:negative-regularity\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:negative-regularity\">43</a>.",
        "source_id": "PM-225.1"
      },
      "evidence": [

          "exact_quote": "If a negative Besov convention is dual to a positive intrinsic Besov difference norm, choose $0<t<s<1$.           "source_id": "PM-225.1"
        }

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-rr-estimates",
        "exact_quote": "Let $N(a,M)$ be the number of loops of diameter at least $a$ meeting $B(0,M)$.         "source_id": "PM-225.1"
      },
      "evidence": [

          "anchor": "stmt-lem-rr-estimates",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-rr-nails",
        "exact_quote": "Fix $\\alpha\\in(0,\\pi)$. <a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **plane law** ([source](#pm-h-3)): We denote the resulting plane law by $\mathbb P_c$.
- **balanced torus sector** ([source](#pm-h-3)): On $$T_{M,L}=(\mathbb Z/M\mathbb Z)\times(\mathbb Z/L\mathbb Z),\qquad M,L\ge4,\quad L\ \text{even},$$ condition every vertical column of horizontal edges to contain $L/2$ left arrows and $L/2$ right arrows.
- **height function** ([source](#pm-h-3)): Give the face on the left of an arrow height one more than the face on its right.
- **Gaussian free field and Green kernel** ([source](#eq:green)): Let $\Gamma$ be the real plane Gaussian free field modulo constants, normalized by the Green kernel $$\label{eq:green} G(x,y)=-\frac1{2\pi}\log\|x-y\|.$$ Thus its covariance on smooth compactly supported tests of total integral zero is the integral of $G$ against their product. For compactly supported finite signed measures $\mu,\nu$ of total mass zero, write $$\mathcal E(\mu,\nu)=\iint G(x,y)\,\mu(\,\mathrm dx)\nu(\,\mathrm dy).$$ We say that $\mu$ has finite energy if $$\iint \log_+\frac1{\|x-y\|}\,\|\mu\|(\,\mathrm dx)\|\mu\|(\,\mathrm dy)<\infty, \qquad \log_+t=\max(\log t,0).$$ For compactly supported finite signed measures, this is equivalent to requiring $\mathcal E(\mu,\mu)$ to be an ordinary finite signed-measure integral. In particular, the definition does not allow cancellation between infinite positive and negative energies.
- **finite energy** ([source](#eq:green)): We say that $\mu$ has finite energy if $$\iint \log_+\frac1{\|x-y\|}\,\|\mu\|(\,\mathrm dx)\|\mu\|(\,\mathrm dy)<\infty, \qquad \log_+t=\max(\log t,0).$$
- **diagonal word subspace** ([source](#pm-h-26)): We now restrict when indicated to the closed diagonal-word subspace $\mathcal D\subset\mathcal H$: it is generated by words all of whose marks are diagonal.
- **orbit mass** ([source](#eq:orbit-disintegration)): On the cross-section where the smallest zero radius is one, it has the form $$\label{eq:orbit-disintegration} \mu=\,\mathrm ds\,\nu(\,\mathrm dF),\qquad f(w)=F(e^s w),\qquad \kappa:=\nu(1)<\infty.$$

| q-kappa-upper | orbit mass upper bound | ≤ | $\frac{\eta}{2\lambda(\pi-\lambda)}$ | below the first threshold |
| q-variogram | height variance | = | $4\kappa\log k+O(1)$ | lattice displacements |
| q-contraction | holomorphic contraction | = | $-\frac{\kappa}{(z-\xi)^2}$ | separated points |
| q-endpoint-multiplier | endpoint squared multiplier | = | $2/\pi$ | endpoint |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **s-row-algebra** [claim](#stmt-lem-row-algebra) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-row-algebra): Local gauge identity and RTT relation. -> gauge, adjoint and commutation rules
- **s-finite-actions** [claim](#stmt-lem-finite-actions) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-finite-actions): Commutation relations applied inductively. -> entry action formulas
- **s-bethe-data** [claim](#stmt-lem-bethe-data) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-bethe-data): Logarithmic differentiation and implicit function theorem. -> Bethe equations and Gaudin Jacobian
- **s-scalar-product** [claim](#stmt-lem-scalar-product) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-scalar-product): Cited scalar-product theorem and pole cancellation. -> determinant formulas
- **s-hilbert-dual** [claim](#stmt-lem-hilbert-dual) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-hilbert-dual): Polarized adjoint identity. -> positive norms and overlaps
- **s-word-expansion** [claim](#stmt-cor-finite-word-expansion) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-finite-word-expansion): Combine actions with deleted-column products. -> path determinant expansion
- **s-root-limits** [claim](#stmt-lem-root-limits) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-root-limits): Convexity continuation and quantile Newton contraction. -> roots and density estimates
- **s-gaudin-inverse** [claim](#stmt-lem-gaudin-inverse) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-gaudin-inverse): Row dominance and compactness at sparse ends. -> uniform inverse bounds
- **s-row-normalization** [claim](#stmt-lem-row-normalization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-row-normalization): Harmonic majorant and strip maximum principle. -> row bound and vacuum modulus
- **s-weighted-flags** [claim](#stmt-lem-weighted-flags) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-weighted-flags): Residue subtraction and rank-nullity. -> nested decay subspaces
- **s-joint-tails** [claim](#stmt-lem-joint-determinant-tails) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-joint-determinant-tails): Flag bases in minors against Cauchy cost. -> exponential tail bound
- **s-word-kernel** [claim](#stmt-prop-word-kernel) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-word-kernel): Window limit then tail removal, normal families. -> holomorphic word kernels
- **s-angular-space** [claim](#stmt-prop-angular-space) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-angular-space): Gram completion and spectral representation. -> angular Hilbert space
- **s-scalar-continuation** [claim](#stmt-lem-scalar-continuation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-scalar-continuation): Folded block division and Taylor continuation. -> inner spectral functions
- **s-physical-plane** [claim](#stmt-prop-physical-plane) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-physical-plane): Perron limit, exterior functional comparison, variational identification. -> plane law identification
- **s-inner-spectra** [claim](#stmt-lem-inner-spectra) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-inner-spectra): Exchange error bounds, factorization, orbit disintegration. -> Blaschke spectra and orbit measure
- **s-low-band** [claim](#stmt-lem-low-band) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-low-band): Zero budget, gluing, Laurent bounds. -> low-band current bounds
- **s-endpoint-generator** [claim](#stmt-lem-endpoint-hamiltonian) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-endpoint-hamiltonian): Differentiate inner factors; analytic vectors. -> local generator identification
- **s-sum-rule** [claim](#stmt-lem-sum-rule) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-sum-rule): Commutator Fourier transform and change of variables. -> sum rule
- **s-variogram** [claim](#stmt-prop-variogram) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-variogram): Telescoping identity and orbit estimates. -> logarithmic variance
- **s-stiffness** [claim](#stmt-lem-stiffness) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-stiffness): Twist derivatives and boundary evaluation. -> stiffness value
- **s-infrared** [claim](#stmt-lem-infrared) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-infrared): Regularized quadratic form comparison. -> lower bound
- **s-overlap** [claim](#stmt-lem-overlap-asymptotic) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-detector-packing-3): Symbol evaluation of bulk Toeplitz traces. -> overlap density
- **s-packing** [claim](#stmt-lem-detector-packing) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-detector-packing): Window packing with two-dip bounds. -> upper bound
- **s-normalization** [claim](#stmt-prop-normalization) / [proof](#stmt-prop-normalization): Match lower and upper bounds. -> orbit mass value
- **s-chiral-forms** [claim](#stmt-lem-chiral-forms) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-chiral-forms): Taylor coefficient of compressed current. -> chiral forms
- **s-two-scale** [claim](#stmt-prop-two-scale) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-two-scale): Exterior zero exclusion and cluster expansion. -> two-scale limit
- **s-current-limit** [claim](#stmt-lem-current-limit) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-current-limit): Contour coefficients and dyadic tail summation. -> current convergence
- **s-chiral-holomorphy** [claim](#stmt-lem-chiral-holomorphy) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-chiral-holomorphy): Damping in axial orderings. -> holomorphic extension
- **s-collision** [claim](#stmt-lem-collision) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-collision): Second scale separation and cutoff removal. -> scalar collision limit
- **s-wick** [claim](#stmt-prop-gaussian-currents) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-gaussian-currents): Pole removal and Liouville argument. -> Wick recursion
- **s-ordinary-moments** [claim](#stmt-lem-ordinary-moments) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-ordinary-moments): Double commutator bounds with the endpoint generator. -> spin sum moments
- **s-neutral-blocks** [claim](#stmt-cor-neutral-blocks) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-neutral-blocks): Summation by parts per column. -> block moments
- **s-smooth-tests** [claim](#stmt-prop-smooth-tests) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-smooth-tests): Line-neutral reduction and dominated convergence. -> smooth test convergence
- **s-separated-block** [claim](#stmt-lem-separated-block) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-separated-block): Damped slab estimate and analytic continuation. -> decoupling bound
- **s-offdiagonal** [claim](#stmt-prop-offdiagonal) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-offdiagonal): Dyadic telescoping to the mesh. -> point increment moments
- **s-log-integrability** [claim](#stmt-lem-log-integrability) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-log-integrability): Dyadic squares and Cauchy–Schwarz. -> absolute energy finiteness
- **s-finite-energy** [claim](#stmt-prop-finite-energy) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-finite-energy): Variogram majorant and mollification. -> finite-energy convergence
- **s-pinned** [claim](#stmt-lem-pinned-averages) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-pinned-averages): Telescoping bumps to the pin. -> logarithmic moment bound
- **s-negative-regularity** [claim](#stmt-prop-negative-regularity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-negative-regularity): Wavelet coefficient moments and compactness. -> tight convergence
- **s-cell-averages** [claim](#stmt-lem-cell-averages) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-cell-averages): Line weight flattening and Jensen. -> cell average bound
- **s-intrinsic-dual** [claim](#stmt-prop-intrinsic-dual) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-intrinsic-dual): Cell martingale tail and energy approximation. -> dual convergence
- **s-rr-bkw** [claim](#stmt-lem-rr-bkw) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-rr-bkw): Loop-orientation correspondence and local convergence. -> gradient law identification
- **s-rr-estimates** [claim](#stmt-lem-rr-estimates) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-rr-estimates): Arm exponent bounds from RSW. -> loop bounds
- **s-rr-nails** [claim](#stmt-lem-rr-nails) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-rr-nails): Track exchange coupling. -> loop bijection
- **s-rr-rotation** [claim](#stmt-thm-rr-rotation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-rr-nails-2): Even-moment reduction and reflection symmetry. -> isometry invariance
- **s-endpoint-topologies** [claim](#stmt-prop-endpoint-topologies) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-endpoint-topologies): Cited logarithmic moment bounds. -> endpoint topologies
- **s-endpoint-completion** [claim](#stmt-prop-endpoint-completion) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-endpoint-completion): Rotation input and cited alternative. -> endpoint theorem
- **s-main-theorem** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-endpoint-topologies-2): Assemble interval and endpoint arguments. -> main theorem

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-multiplier-formula**: For every fixed c in (0,2], the rescaled height field converges to a multiple of the plane Gaussian free field whose squared multiplier is the closed form 1/arcsin(c/2), equal to 2/arccos of the anisotropy parameter.
- **inv-orbit-mass-below-one**: In the regime 0<c<1 the orbit mass kappa equals 1/(2 pi (pi - lambda)), so the squared multiplier 4 pi kappa equals 2/(pi - lambda).
- **inv-endpoint-multiplier**: At the endpoint c=2 the conclusions of the main theorem hold with squared multiplier 2/pi.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-other-weights**: Apply the multiplier to six-vertex models with unequal horizontal and vertical weights or outside the critical range. -> The multiplier formula was derived only for the symmetric critical weights; other weights give a different or unproved limit.
- **anti-finite-domain**: Use the whole-plane Gaussian free field law and its multiplier for a finite domain with fixed boundary data. -> The law is an infinite-volume limit of balanced tori and says nothing about boundary-constrained finite domains.
- **anti-all-gibbs-states**: Claim the Gaussian free field limit for every Gibbs state or every slope, or uniformly as c tends to zero. -> The theorem concerns one particular zero-slope state and gives no uniqueness or uniform-in-c estimate.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The results are asymptotic convergence-in-law and existence theorems for infinite-volume limits as the mesh tends to zero; they supply no finite procedure with a computable output beyond closed-form constants, so an executable reference and independent oracle would only restate a formula and cannot test the limit statements.

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper proves existence and scaling limits of a plane state and gives no executable procedure or interface to implement; its finite Bethe-root formulas are only intermediate tools for limit arguments. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The Gaussian free field limit describes macroscopic height statistics of one zero-slope state, so it could only serve as a statistical sanity target for a sampler. The paper supplies no sampling algorithm. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No tool, checker or reusable utility is defined. The oracle-style checks possible here would only test isolated numeric identities, not the theorem. |
| algorithms | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The root-existence and quadrature arguments use fixed-point and contraction steps inside proofs, but they are asymptotic and non-effective with unspecified constants. They do not form a stated algorithm with a complexity. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper introduces no data structure. Its Hilbert space, word kernels and spectral measures are analytic objects used only in limit proofs. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Nothing in the results concerns hardware, parallelism or numerical precision. The bounds hold only in the asymptotic regimes stated in the lemmas. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No hardness assumption, key, or adversary model appears; the correlations studied are physical height statistics, not pseudorandom or secret quantities. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No learning problem, loss or generalisation claim appears. Convergence in negative-regularity spaces is a statement about random fields, not about trained models. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | A free-energy variational characterisation is used to identify the state, but no optimisation problem is solved or posed for a decision-maker, and no solution method is given. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No protocol, consistency or fault model appears. The row transfer-matrix structure is a mathematical device rather than a distributed computation. |
| other | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The result belongs to mathematical statistical physics and probability: a scaling limit of one lattice height field. It is not an engineering contract, and the paper itself restricts it to a particular zero-slope state. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-zero-slope-state-only** (formal_scope): The theorem describes one specific zero-slope plane state. It asserts no uniqueness among Gibbs states or slopes, and no estimate that is uniform as the weight parameter tends to zero. Blocked use: ['Claiming the Gaussian free field limit for tilted or non-zero-slope Gibbs states', 'Using the multiplier formula with a constant that is uniform in c near zero', 'Using the result to assert uniqueness of the six-vertex plane Gibbs state']
- **limit-critical-weight-range** (hypothesis): The statement is proved only for the symmetric critical weights a=b=1 and the stated range of c. It gives no information for asymmetric horizontal and vertical weights or for weights outside that range. Blocked use: ['Applying the multiplier to anisotropic models with different horizontal and vertical weights', 'Extrapolating the formula to c above the stated range']
- **limit-iterated-torus-order** (hypothesis): The plane law is defined by a prescribed order of limits: first the horizontal period to infinity, then the even vertical period, in a balanced sector. Other orders of limits or other sectors are not covered. Blocked use: ['Exchanging the order of the thermodynamic limits and quoting the same law', 'Using torus sectors with an unbalanced number of left and right arrows per column', 'Applying the law to finite domains with fixed boundary data']
- **limit-finite-bethe-no-uniform-bound** (formal_scope): The finite Bethe-data lemma concerns finite matrices only. It does not give an inverse bound for the weighted Gaudin matrix that is uniform in the chain length; that bound requires the separate thermodynamic estimates. Blocked use: ['Taking the chain length to infinity using only the finite positivity or invertibility statements', 'Quoting the finite lemma as a uniform conditioning guarantee for numerical root solvers']
- **limit-constants-parameter-dependent** (approximation): All constants in the thermodynamic estimates depend on the fixed spectral parameter and may degenerate at both ends of its open interval. The bounds are asymptotic and ineffective, so they carry no explicit rates. Blocked use: ['Reading the estimates as quantitative error bounds for finite lattices', 'Using the constants uniformly as the spectral parameter approaches either end of its range']
- **limit-no-bethe-completeness** (formal_scope): The finite-row algebra makes no completeness claim for Bethe eigenvectors. It gives expansions of fixed words in terms of the constructed root lists only. Blocked use: ['Using the Bethe vectors as a basis of the whole transfer-matrix space', 'Deducing the full spectrum of the row transfer matrix from the constructed ground state']
- **limit-orbit-mass-below-one-only** (hypothesis): The explicit evaluation of the orbit mass, via matching lower and upper bounds, is carried out only in the regime below the first threshold of the weight parameter. The stiffness, infrared and packing arguments are stated only there. Blocked use: ['Using the explicit formula for the orbit mass at weights at or above the first threshold', 'Reusing the array and stiffness comparison where the kernel changes sign']
- **limit-multiplier-conditional-middle-range** (nonconstructive): For the middle range of the weight parameter the multiplier is not computed by the paper. It is taken from a cited conditional theorem that is applied only after convergence has been proved. Blocked use: ['Presenting the multiplier in the middle range as independently derived by the lattice arguments', 'Using the cited theorem to supply the convergence itself']
- **limit-endpoint-topologies-conditional** (hypothesis): At the endpoint, the transfer to restriction, outward and intrinsic-dual topologies is conditional on joint convergence of finite-energy neutral pairings. Without that input the implication does not hold. Blocked use: ['Deducing convergence in negative Besov, Sobolev or intrinsic-dual spaces at the endpoint from loop estimates alone', 'Applying the implication to families of measures outside the finite-energy neutral class']

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove that the height function of the square-lattice six-vertex model with weights $`a=b=1`$ and $`0\lt c\le2`$, in the plane state obtained from balanced tori, converges to a multiple of the Gaussian free field. For unit height jumps and Green kernel $`-(2\pi)^{-1}\log|x-y|`$, the squared multiplier is $`1/\arcsin(c/2)`$.

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### Introduction

The six-vertex model assigns an arrow to each edge of the square lattice, subject to the rule that two arrows enter and two arrows leave each vertex. Its height function is a discrete random surface. We prove the Gaussian free field limit of this surface throughout the critical range $a=b=1$, $0<c\le2$, for the plane state specified below.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### The plane law and the height convention

The two vertices having both horizontal arrows directed inward, or both directed outward, have weight $c$; the other four vertices have weight one. A finite configuration has the product of its vertex weights. On $$T_{M,L}=(\mathbb Z/M\mathbb Z)\times(\mathbb Z/L\mathbb Z),\qquad M,L\ge4,\quad L\ \text{even},$$ condition every vertical column of horizontal edges to contain $L/2$ left arrows and $L/2$ right arrows. This is a conserved sector of the row transfer matrix. We first take $M\to\infty$, and then even $L\to\infty$, in the local weak topology of arrow configurations. We denote the resulting plane law by $\mathbb P_c$. Existence in the previously untreated range is part of the theorem.

Give the face on the left of an arrow height one more than the face on its right. The ice rule makes this a well-defined height $h$, modulo an additive constant, on the faces of the plane. For $x=(x_1,x_2)\in\mathbb R^2$, let $h(x)$ be the height of the face with bottom-left corner $(\lfloor x_1\rfloor,\lfloor x_2\rfloor)$, and set $$h_\delta(x)=h(x/\delta),\qquad \delta>0.$$ This convention also fixes the value on grid lines.

<a id="eq:green"></a>
Let $\Gamma$ be the real plane Gaussian free field modulo constants, normalized by the Green kernel $$\label{eq:green}
 G(x,y)=-\frac1{2\pi}\log|x-y|.$$ Thus its covariance on smooth compactly supported tests of total integral zero is the integral of $G$ against their product. For compactly supported finite signed measures $\mu,\nu$ of total mass zero, write $$\mathcal E(\mu,\nu)=\iint G(x,y)\,\mu(\,\mathrm dx)\nu(\,\mathrm dy).$$ We say that $\mu$ has finite energy if $$\iint \log_+\frac1{|x-y|}\,|\mu|(\,\mathrm dx)|\mu|(\,\mathrm dy)<\infty,
 \qquad \log_+t=\max(\log t,0).$$ For compactly supported finite signed measures, this is equivalent to requiring $\mathcal E(\mu,\mu)$ to be an ordinary finite signed-measure integral. In particular, the definition does not allow cancellation between infinite positive and negative energies.

<a id="stmt-thm-main"></a>
<div id="thm:main" class="theorem">

<a id="eq:multiplier"></a>
**Theorem 1**. *For every fixed $c\in(0,2]$, the prescribed iterated balanced-torus limit $\mathbb P_c$ exists. Define $$\label{eq:multiplier}
 \Delta=1-\frac{c^2}{2},\qquad
 \sigma(c)^2=\frac2{\arccos\Delta}=\frac1{\arcsin(c/2)}.$$ As $\delta\downarrow0$ through arbitrary positive real values, the fields $h_\delta$ converge to $\sigma(c)\Gamma$ in each of the following senses.*

1.  *For every finite family $\mu_1,\ldots,\mu_m$ of compactly supported finite signed measures of total mass zero and finite energy, $$\bigl(\langle h_\delta,\mu_j\rangle\bigr)_{j=1}^m
     \ \Longrightarrow\
     \mathcal N\!\left(0,
           \bigl(\sigma(c)^2\mathcal E(\mu_i,\mu_j)\bigr)_{i,j=1}^m\right).$$*

2.  *For every bounded open $U\subset\mathbb R^2$ and $-1<\alpha<0$, there is convergence in law, with tightness, in the ordinary restriction spaces $$\mathcal C^\alpha_{\mathrm{res}}(U):=B^\alpha_{\infty,\infty}(U),
     \qquad B^\alpha_{p,q}(U),\qquad W^{\alpha,p}(U),
     \quad 1\le p<\infty,\quad 1\le q\le\infty,$$ modulo constants. Convergence also holds in the outward completion <a href="#eq:source-holder" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:source-holder">[eq:source-holder]</a> defined below. No regularity of $\partial U$ is required. One may fix a smooth compactly supported $\rho$ of integral one and replace each field $X$ by $X-\langle X,\rho\rangle$, using the same pinning for the limit.*

<a id="eq:increment-main"></a>
3.  *For every $k\ge1$ and every tuple $(u_i,u_i')_{i=1}^k$ with $\{u_i,u_i'\}\cap\{u_j,u_j'\}=\varnothing$ for $i\ne j$, $$\label{eq:increment-main}
     \mathbb E_c\prod_{i=1}^k
        \bigl(h_\delta(u_i')-h_\delta(u_i)\bigr)
     \longrightarrow
     \sigma(c)^k\sum_{\pi}\prod_{\{i,j\}\in\pi}C_{ij},$$ where the sum is over pairings of $\{1,\ldots,k\}$, is zero for odd $k$, and $$C_{ij}=G(u_i',u_j')-G(u_i',u_j)-G(u_i,u_j')+G(u_i,u_j).$$ The convergence is uniform on compact sets of such tuples.*

</div>

The theorem concerns this particular zero-slope plane state. It makes no uniqueness assertion among all Gibbs states or all slopes, and no estimate uniform as $c\downarrow0$.

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
###### Regularity conventions.

The restriction spaces in part 2 are restrictions of the corresponding whole-plane distribution spaces, with their quotient norms. For $W^{\alpha,p}$ we include both the Bessel-potential and the negative Slobodeckij realizations. The Hölder laws are supported on the separable closure of smooth functions. This choice makes the statement meaningful on every bounded open set without an extension theorem for its boundary. We also prove convergence in the intrinsic dual spaces $(W^{s,p'}(U))^*$, for $0<s<1$ and $1\le p<\infty$, where the fractional seminorm uses only pairs of points of $U$; see Proposition <a href="#prop:intrinsic-dual" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:intrinsic-dual">45</a>.

<a id="eq:source-holder"></a>
The outward convention is the literal seminorm of (Duminil-Copin, Kozlowski, Lammers, et al. 2026, Definition 2.6(iii)) <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[DKLM2026](#ref-DKLM2026)]. Let $\mathcal T_1(Q)$, with $Q=(-1,1)^2$, consist of compactly supported signed densities in $Q$, of integral zero, whose zero extensions are $1$-Lipschitz. Set $$\label{eq:source-holder}
 \|f\|_{\mathrm{out},\alpha,U}
 =\sup_{\substack{0<\varepsilon\le1,\ \varphi\in\mathcal T_1(Q)\\
                   \mathop{\mathrm{supp}}\varphi\subset U/\varepsilon}}
   \varepsilon^{-\alpha}
   \left|\int f(x/\varepsilon)\varphi(x)\,\,\mathrm dx\right|.$$ Here $U$ indexes the allowed tests of the global plane field; the sampled points $x/\varepsilon$ need not lie in $U$. The space is the completion of $C_c^\infty(\mathbb R^2)$ modulo the kernel of this seminorm. The absolute value leaves the source convention unchanged because the test family is symmetric. Its convergence requires a separate argument from convergence in restriction spaces.

<a id="pm-h-5"></a>
#### Prior work and the new ingredients

At $c=1$, Lieb computed the exact square-ice entropy (Lieb 1967) <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[Lieb1967](#ref-Lieb1967)]. The Gaussian description of critical six-vertex heights and the prediction of its coupling belong to the Coulomb-gas approach. In the F-model regime $1<c<2$, Di Francesco, Saleur and Zuber (di Francesco, Saleur, and Zuber 1987, sec. 2.3) <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[DFSZ1987](#ref-DFSZ1987)] give a Gaussian-action prediction which, after converting their height steps of $\pi/2$ to unit steps, agrees with <a href="#eq:multiplier" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:multiplier">[eq:multiplier]</a>. This physics prediction is distinct from convergence in the measure classes and distribution topologies of Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>.

At the free-fermion point $c=\sqrt2$, a dimer representation makes the correlations determinantal. Kenyon’s conformal-invariance and GFF theorems for domino tilings (Kenyon 2000, 2001) <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[Kenyon2000](#ref-Kenyon2000), [Kenyon2001](#ref-Kenyon2001)] are foundational scaling-limit results in this setting. The periodic dimer Gibbs measures, surface tension and logarithmic fluctuations studied by Kenyon, Okounkov and Sheffield (Kenyon, Okounkov, and Sheffield 2006) <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[KOS2006](#ref-KOS2006)] describe the corresponding plane-state picture. Near the free-fermion point, Falco (Falco 2013) <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[Falco2013](#ref-Falco2013)] derived arrow-correlation asymptotics and logarithmic height variance for the isotropic six-vertex model through an interacting-fermion representation. Giuliani, Mastropietro and Toninelli proved GFF convergence for a symmetric weakly interacting dimer model (Giuliani, Mastropietro, and Toninelli 2017b) <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[GMT2017](#ref-GMT2017)] and the relation between its height amplitude and correlation exponent (Giuliani, Mastropietro, and Toninelli 2017a) <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[GMTHaldane2017](#ref-GMTHaldane2017)]. Their later treatment of tilted profiles (Giuliani, Mastropietro, and Toninelli 2020, sec. 2.3 and Theorem 2) <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[GMT2020](#ref-GMT2020)] includes the six-vertex interaction and establishes height-covariance and amplitude identities near the free-fermion point; its Remark 5 leaves the higher-cumulant extension needed for a full GFF theorem undeveloped.

Duminil-Copin, Kozlowski, Krachun, Manolescu and Tikhonovskaia (Duminil-Copin et al. 2022) <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[DCKKMT2022](#ref-DCKKMT2022)] proved existence and condensation of Bethe roots and obtained the free energy and its expansion near half filling. Duminil-Copin, Karrila, Manolescu and Oulamara (Duminil-Copin et al. 2024) <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[DCKMO2024](#ref-DCKMO2024)] proved logarithmic delocalization for $1\le c\le2$, using crossing estimates and free-energy information. Lis (Lis 2021) <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[Lis2021](#ref-Lis2021)] developed the Baxter–Kelland–Wu representation for multi-point spin correlations and proved infinite-volume and delocalization results on subintervals of this range. Glazman and Lammers (Glazman and Lammers 2025) <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[GlazmanLammers2025](#ref-GlazmanLammers2025)] subsequently obtained delocalization, including logarithmic bounds in the isotropic case, by percolation methods. These results establish the scale of fluctuations; identifying their joint law requires additional information. The variational framework of Sheffield (Sheffield 2005) <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[Sheffield2005](#ref-Sheffield2005)] and the strict convexity theorem of Lammers and Tassy (Lammers and Tassy 2024) <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[LammersTassy2024](#ref-LammersTassy2024)] provide the equilibrium tools used below when $c\ge1$.

Duminil-Copin, Kozlowski, Lammers and Manolescu (Duminil-Copin, Kozlowski, Lammers, et al. 2026) <a id="pm-cite-16"></a>[[DKLM2026](#ref-DKLM2026)] give a nonperturbative GFF theorem on $\sqrt3\le c\le2$, with the exact multiplier <a href="#eq:multiplier" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:multiplier">[eq:multiplier]</a>. Their proof combines transfer-matrix spectral representations and reflection positivity with rotational invariance and regularity estimates. The rotational-invariance input is listed as in preparation in the cited version. We retain the use of transfer spectra and reflection, but for $0<c<2$ construct the two Gaussian currents from finite row identities and uniform thermodynamic estimates. At $c=2$, Appendix <a href="#sec:endpoint-rotation" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:endpoint-rotation">9</a> supplies the needed rotational-invariance statement from the independent fair orientations of FK loops, the plane loop correspondence of (Lis 2021) <a id="pm-cite-17"></a>[[Lis2021](#ref-Lis2021)], and the homotopy coupling underlying the FK rotation theorem of (Duminil-Copin, Kozlowski, Krachun, et al. 2026) <a id="pm-cite-18"></a>[[DCKKMO2026Rotation](#ref-DCKKMO2026Rotation)]. The remaining endpoint argument uses (Duminil-Copin, Kozlowski, Lammers, et al. 2026) <a id="pm-cite-19"></a>[[DKLM2026](#ref-DKLM2026)]. Its conditional amplitude theorem is also used on $1\le c<2$, after convergence has been established. Thus the full-range statement concerns precisely the balanced plane law defined above, with its normalization fixed in physical height units.

The finite algebra is the algebraic Bethe ansatz. The norm determinant goes back to Gaudin, McCoy and Wu (Gaudin, McCoy, and Wu 1981) <a id="pm-cite-20"></a>[[GaudinMcCoyWu1981](#ref-GaudinMcCoyWu1981)] and Korepin (Korepin 1982) <a id="pm-cite-21"></a>[[Korepin1982](#ref-Korepin1982)]; the scalar-product formula is due to Slavnov (Slavnov 1989) <a id="pm-cite-22"></a>[[Slavnov1989](#ref-Slavnov1989)]. We use its trigonometric and diagonally twisted forms as developed by Kitanine, Maillet and Terras (Kitanine, Maillet, and Terras 1999) <a id="pm-cite-23"></a>[[KMT1999](#ref-KMT1999)] and Kitanine, Maillet, Slavnov and Terras (Kitanine et al. 2005) <a id="pm-cite-24"></a>[[KMST2005](#ref-KMST2005)]. These exact finite identities are the algebraic input. Uniform control as the row length grows is a separate analytical problem, addressed in Section <a href="#sec:thermodynamic" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:thermodynamic">3</a>.

Three estimates connect this finite algebra to the limiting field. Root-tail control and an edge maximum principle give uniform inverses for Gaudin matrices and for mixed discrete–continuous quadrature operators. Finite-dimensional residue conditions then control simultaneous escapes of several rapidities in the determinant formulas. Next, bounds on the zeros of angular spectral functions outside two separated scale windows justify the limiting holomorphic and antiholomorphic currents. Finally, when $c<1$, a homogeneous stiffness lower bound and a staggered vacuum-overlap upper bound identify the same covariance coefficient. This last step supplies the normalization where the conditional probabilistic input is unavailable. Moment estimates and desmoothing pass from the currents to the test classes and separated increments in Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>.

<a id="sec:overview"></a>
#### Proof overview

For $0<c<2$, introduce $$\lambda=\arccos(-\Delta)\in(0,\pi),
 \qquad \eta=\lambda/\pi.$$ Section <a href="#sec:finite" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:finite">2</a> defines commuting row operators and their diagonal marks, which record height increments. Section <a href="#sec:thermodynamic" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:thermodynamic">3</a> constructs their limiting matrix elements, proves uniform tail bounds, and shows that words with widely separated angular parameters decorrelate. A positive full-line resolvent gives uniform density comparisons for every fixed positive $\lambda$, including the small-$\lambda$ range.

These matrix elements define a Hilbert space with commuting angular rows in Section <a href="#sec:angular" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:angular">4</a>. Their joint spectral values are inner functions of a complex row parameter. The finite folded-row continuation argument in Lemma <a href="#lem:scalar-continuation" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:scalar-continuation">16</a> gives a common analytic neighborhood; canonical inner-function factorization describes the possible zeros and boundary singularities (Ai, Dong, and Jiang 2019) <a id="pm-cite-25"></a>[[AiDongJiang2019](#ref-AiDongJiang2019)]. A mark acts as a current. The finite mass $\kappa$ of the spectrum transverse to common angular dilation is the coefficient of the logarithmic height covariance. Section <a href="#sec:normalization" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:normalization">5</a> computes $\kappa$ directly when $c<1$; for $c\ge1$ the cited conditional normalization theorem applies once convergence has been established.

Section <a href="#sec:chiral" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:chiral">6</a> separates the angular spectrum into two scales and proves the Wick rules for the limiting currents. Section <a href="#sec:probability" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:probability">7</a> passes from these currents to smooth tests, separated point increments and finite-energy measures, then proves convergence in negative regularity spaces. Its wavelet argument uses compactly supported wavelets (Daubechies 1988) <a id="pm-cite-26"></a>[[Daubechies1988](#ref-Daubechies1988)] and the classical Besov coefficient characterization in the form of (Hairer and Labbé 2017, Proposition 2.3) <a id="pm-cite-27"></a>[[HairerLabbe2017](#ref-HairerLabbe2017)]; restriction from the plane avoids any regularity assumption on the boundary of $U$. The final proof combines the interval $0<c<2$ with the endpoint argument of Appendix <a href="#sec:endpoint-rotation" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:endpoint-rotation">9</a>. Each thermodynamic passage specifies which size tends to infinity first.

<a id="sec:finite"></a>
### Finite rows and their scalar products

The first step is finite-dimensional. We express every fixed word of marked rows as a finite sum of determinants on the Bethe roots, with all normalizing factors specified. The construction of those roots and the estimates needed to pass to infinite volume are deferred to Section <a href="#sec:thermodynamic" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:thermodynamic">3</a>. In particular, no completeness assertion for Bethe eigenvectors is used in this section.

<a id="pm-h-8"></a>
#### The physical row and the standard monodromy

The algebra below holds for $0<\lambda<\pi$, corresponding to the open parameter interval $0<c<2$. The endpoint $c=2$ is treated separately in Appendix <a href="#sec:endpoint-rotation" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:endpoint-rotation">9</a>. Set <a id="eq:spectral-coordinates"></a>$$\label{eq:spectral-coordinates}
 \lambda=\arccos(-\Delta),\qquad \eta=\lambda/\pi,\qquad
 v=\pi/2,\qquad x=i\eta z,\qquad w=e^{iz}.$$ The physical time angle is $z=0$, increasing time points east, and the space angle is $v$, increasing space points north. Thus a common imaginary translation of the $z$ arguments is a common real translation of their $x$ arguments. At a path step, the spin is $+1$ when the arrow points along its left normal; the height increment is minus that spin. These conventions fix the sign of every marked row.

<a id="eq:physical-R"></a>
Let $L$ be even and let $b_0,\ldots,b_{L-1}$ be real numbers. On $\mathcal H_L=(\mathbb C^2)^{\otimes L}$ use the orthonormal spin basis, and put $$Z=\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\0&-1\end{pmatrix},\qquad
 X=\begin{pmatrix}0&1\\1&0\end{pmatrix}.$$ An auxiliary copy of $\mathbb C^2$ is denoted by the subscript $a$. Products over the sites below have increasing site index from left to right. Define <a id="eq:physical-row"></a>$$\begin{aligned}
 R(u)&=
 \begin{pmatrix}
 \sin(\lambda-u)/\sin\lambda&0&0&0\\
 0&\sin u/\sin\lambda&1&0\\
 0&1&\sin u/\sin\lambda&0\\
 0&0&0&\sin(\lambda-u)/\sin\lambda
 \end{pmatrix},\label{eq:physical-R}\\
 M_{\rm ph}(x)&=\prod_{j=0}^{L-1}
 R_{aj}\bigl(\lambda/2+i(x-b_j)\bigr),\qquad
 T^D(x)=\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}_a\bigl(D_aM_{\rm ph}(x)\bigr),\label{eq:physical-row}
\end{aligned}$$ where $D$ is an arbitrary complex $2\times2$ matrix. Its row index is on the right tile of the horizontal seam and its column index on the left. The four indices in <a href="#eq:physical-R" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:physical-R">[eq:physical-R]</a> send the top and left step spins to the bottom and right step spins. When $b_j=0$ and $x=0$, dividing all vertex weights by $\sin(\lambda/2)/\sin\lambda$ gives $a=b=1$ and $c=2\cos(\lambda/2)$, hence $\Delta=-\cos\lambda$.

<a id="eq:standard-monodromy"></a>
Write $[u]=\sinh u$ and $s_\lambda=[i\lambda]=i\sin\lambda$. The standard monodromy used in the algebraic Bethe ansatz is $$\begin{aligned}
 L_{aj}(x-b_j)&=
 \begin{pmatrix}
 [x-b_j+i\lambda/2]/s_\lambda&0&0&0\\
 0&[x-b_j-i\lambda/2]/s_\lambda&1&0\\
 0&1&[x-b_j-i\lambda/2]/s_\lambda&0\\
 0&0&0&[x-b_j+i\lambda/2]/s_\lambda
 \end{pmatrix},\notag\\
 M(x)&=\prod_{j=0}^{L-1}L_{aj}(x-b_j)
      =\begin{pmatrix}\mathsf A(x)&\mathsf B(x)\\
                         \mathsf C(x)&\mathsf L(x)\end{pmatrix}_a.
 \label{eq:standard-monodromy}
\end{aligned}$$ In particular, the physical mixed diagonal entry has the opposite sign. Let <a id="eq:parity-gauges"></a>$$\label{eq:parity-gauges}
 \mathcal P_L=\prod_{j\ {\mathrm{even}}}Z_j,
 \qquad \mathcal Q_L=\prod_{j=0}^{L-1}Z_j.$$

<a id="stmt-lem-row-algebra"></a>
<div id="lem:row-algebra" class="lemma">

<a id="eq:monodromy-gauge"></a>
<a id="eq:physical-RTT"></a>
**Lemma 2** (Finite row algebra). *For even $L$, real $b_j$, and all complex spectral arguments, $$\label{eq:monodromy-gauge}
 M_{\rm ph}(x)=\mathcal P_L M(x)\mathcal P_L\mathcal Q_L,
 \qquad
 T^D(x)^*=T^{X\overline D X}(\bar x).$$ The rows with a common diagonal twist commute. More generally, put $$\mathcal R(u)=
 \begin{pmatrix}
 [u+i\lambda]/s_\lambda&0&0&0\\
 0&[u]/s_\lambda&1&0\\
 0&1&[u]/s_\lambda&0\\
 0&0&0&[u+i\lambda]/s_\lambda
 \end{pmatrix},\qquad
 \mathcal R_{\rm ph}(u)=Z_a\mathcal R(u)Z_b.$$ Then $$\label{eq:physical-RTT}
 \mathcal R_{{\rm ph},ab}(p-q)M_{{\rm ph},a}(p)M_{{\rm ph},b}(q)
 =M_{{\rm ph},b}(q)M_{{\rm ph},a}(p)
   \mathcal R_{{\rm ph},ab}(p-q).$$ Where the intertwiner is invertible, exchanging two rows conjugates their joint auxiliary mark by $\mathcal R_{\rm ph}(p-q)$. For the homogeneous chain, $T^I(i\lambda/2)$ is the cyclic spin translation and $T^I(-i\lambda/2)$ is its inverse.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-row-algebra"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-row-algebra</summary>

*Proof.* The local identity is $R_{aj}(\lambda/2+i(x-b_j))=Z_aL_{aj}(x-b_j)Z_j$. Both local matrices commute with $Z_aZ_j$. Moving the auxiliary $Z$’s through the product, and using that $L$ is even, gives <a href="#eq:monodromy-gauge" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:monodromy-gauge">[eq:monodromy-gauge]</a>. This also proves that the gauge is independent of the roots, spectral arguments, and inhomogeneities.

If $N_{\alpha\beta}(x)$ is one auxiliary block of a physical local matrix, direct conjugation of its entries gives $N_{\alpha\beta}(x)^*=N_{\bar\alpha\bar\beta}(\bar x)$, where a bar on a spin index exchanges its two values. In an entry of a monodromy, different local factors act on different quantum sites. They therefore commute when the adjoint reverses their order. Consequently $$(M_{{\rm ph},\alpha\beta}(x))^*
 =M_{{\rm ph},\bar\alpha\bar\beta}(\bar x).$$ Taking the marked trace proves the second identity in <a href="#eq:monodromy-gauge" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:monodromy-gauge">[eq:monodromy-gauge]</a>; no reversal of the spatial inhomogeneities is involved.

The standard six-vertex Yang–Baxter identity gives $\mathcal R_{ab}(p-q)M_a(p)M_b(q)
=M_b(q)M_a(p)\mathcal R_{ab}(p-q)$. It is a local identity of the three displayed six-vertex matrices: spin conservation reduces its nonconstant entries to the addition formula for $\sinh$. Moreover, $$M_{{\rm ph},a}(p)M_{{\rm ph},b}(q)
 =Z_b\mathcal P_L M_a(p)M_b(q)\mathcal P_L Z_b,$$ and the analogous reversed product has $Z_a$ in place of $Z_b$. Substitution yields <a href="#eq:physical-RTT" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:physical-RTT">[eq:physical-RTT]</a>. Taking auxiliary traces proves the assertion about marks. For a common diagonal matrix $D$, $D\otimes D$ commutes with the intertwiner, so the two twisted rows commute. Finally $R(0)$ is the permutation matrix, and tracing a chain of such permutations gives the cyclic translation. The other endpoint follows by the adjoint identity. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-9"></a>
#### Bethe vectors, counting functions, and finite regularity

<a id="eq:fgbeta"></a>
<a id="eq:bethe-eigenvalue"></a>
<a id="eq:bethe-equations"></a>
Let $|0\rangle=|+\rangle^{\otimes L}$. It is annihilated by $\mathsf C(x)$, and its two diagonal eigenvalues are <a id="eq:vacuum-functions"></a>$$\label{eq:vacuum-functions}
 a(x)=\prod_{j=0}^{L-1}\frac{[x-b_j+i\lambda/2]}{s_\lambda},
 \qquad
 l(x)=\prod_{j=0}^{L-1}\frac{[x-b_j-i\lambda/2]}{s_\lambda}.$$ The scalar functions in the creation-entry commutation rules are $$\label{eq:fgbeta}
 f(u)=\frac{[u+i\lambda]}{[u]},\qquad
 g(u)=\frac{s_\lambda}{[u]},\qquad
 \beta(u)=\frac{g(u)}{f(u)}=\frac{s_\lambda}{[u+i\lambda]}.$$ In particular $\beta(0)=1$ and $1/f(0)=0$ by continuation. For a finite list $U$ set $|U\rangle=\prod_{u\in U}\mathsf B(u)|0\rangle$. The creation entries commute, so this vector is symmetric in $U$. Fix an integer $0\le n\le L$. For a list $Y=(y_1,\ldots,y_n)$ of distinct roots define <a id="eq:bethe-functions"></a>$$\begin{aligned}
 F_Y(x)&=\prod_{y\in Y}\frac{[y-x+i\lambda]}{[y-x]},\notag\\
 q_Y(x)&=\frac{l(x)}{a(x)}
          \prod_{y\in Y}\frac{[x-y+i\lambda]}{[x-y-i\lambda]},
 \label{eq:bethe-functions}\\
 \tau_t(x;Y)&=e^{it}a(x)F_Y(x)
                   \bigl(1+e^{-2it}q_Y(x)\bigr).
 \label{eq:bethe-eigenvalue}
\end{aligned}$$ Here $t$ is real near zero. We say that $Y$ is on shell for $t$ when $$\label{eq:bethe-equations}
 q_Y(y_j)=-e^{2it}\qquad(1\le j\le n).$$ The apparently singular eigenvalue in <a href="#eq:bethe-eigenvalue" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:bethe-eigenvalue">[eq:bethe-eigenvalue]</a> then has removable poles at the roots.

<a id="eq:consecutive-counting"></a>
For real $u$ put <a id="eq:bare-kernels"></a>$$\label{eq:bare-kernels}
 s(u)=\frac{\sin\lambda}{\pi(\cosh(2u)-\cos\lambda)},\qquad
 K(u)=\frac{\sin(2\lambda)}
               {\pi(\cosh(2u)-\cos(2\lambda))}.$$ At $\lambda=\pi/2$, the second function is identically zero. Let $S$ and $\Theta$ be their odd primitives, vanishing at zero. Define <a id="eq:counting-function"></a>$$\label{eq:counting-function}
 \xi_Y(u)=\sum_{j=0}^{L-1}S(u-b_j)-\sum_{k=1}^n\Theta(u-y_k).$$ Consecutive real roots are solutions of [(eq:consecutive-counting)](#eq:consecutive-counting)$$\label{eq:consecutive-counting}
 \xi_Y(y_j)=j-\frac{n+1}{2}+\frac t\pi,
 \qquad y_1<\cdots<y_n.$$

<a id="stmt-lem-bethe-data"></a>
<div id="lem:bethe-data" class="lemma">

<a id="eq:Gaudin-matrix"></a>
<a id="eq:twist-root-derivative"></a>
<a id="eq:Gaudin-weights"></a>
**Lemma 3** (Finite Bethe data). *Let $L$ be even, $b_j$ real, and $0<\lambda<\pi$. A real solution of <a href="#eq:consecutive-counting" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:consecutive-counting">[eq:consecutive-counting]</a> satisfies <a href="#eq:bethe-equations" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:bethe-equations">[eq:bethe-equations]</a>. If its Bethe vector is nonzero, then $|Y\rangle$ is an eigenvector of $e^{it}\mathsf A(x)+e^{-it}\mathsf L(x)$ with eigenvalue $\tau_t(x;Y)$, and $\mathcal P_L|Y\rangle$ is an eigenvector of the physical twisted row with eigenvalue $(-1)^n\tau_t(x;Y)$. The Jacobian of <a href="#eq:consecutive-counting" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:consecutive-counting">[eq:consecutive-counting]</a> is $$\label{eq:Gaudin-matrix}
 G_{jk}=\xi_Y'(y_j)\delta_{jk}+K(y_j-y_k),\qquad
 \xi_Y'(u)=\sum_{a=0}^{L-1}s(u-b_a)-\sum_{k=1}^nK(u-y_k).$$ If $\det G\ne0$, the roots have a real analytic continuation for real $t$ near the given value, and on that branch $$\label{eq:twist-root-derivative}
 G\frac{\,\mathrm dY}{\,\mathrm dt}=\frac1\pi\mathbf 1.$$ For $\lambda\ge\pi/2$, $G$ is positive definite at every real root list. Whenever $\xi_Y'(y_j)>0$, write $$\label{eq:Gaudin-weights}
 \ell_j=\frac1{\xi_Y'(y_j)},\qquad
 (K\ell)_{jk}=K(y_j-y_k)\ell_k;
 \qquad G\mathop{\mathrm{diag}}(\ell)=I+K\ell.$$ These assertions concern finite matrices; they give no uniform bound on $(I+K\ell)^{-1}$ as $L$ increases.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-bethe-data"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-bethe-data</summary>

*Proof.* Logarithmic differentiation gives $q_Y'/q_Y=2\pi i\xi_Y'$. Comparing the limits as $u\to+\infty$ fixes the integration constant: <a id="eq:q-counting-phase"></a>$$\label{eq:q-counting-phase}
 q_Y(u)=(-1)^n e^{2\pi i\xi_Y(u)}\qquad(u\in\mathbb R).$$ Indeed $S(+\infty)=(1-\eta)/2$ and $\Theta(+\infty)=(1-2\eta)/2$, while $q_Y(+\infty)=e^{-i\lambda L+2i\lambda n}$ and $L$ is even. Equation <a href="#eq:consecutive-counting" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:consecutive-counting">[eq:consecutive-counting]</a> now gives the Bethe equations. The two diagonal action formulas proved in Lemma <a href="#lem:finite-actions" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:finite-actions">4</a> below give the coefficient replacing a root $p$ by $x$ as $$g(x-p)\left(
 e^{it}a(p)\prod_{y\ne p}f(y-p)
 -e^{-it}l(p)\prod_{y\ne p}f(p-y)\right).$$ The Bethe equation, with the self factor $-1$ removed, makes the parenthesis zero. The unchanged coefficient is <a href="#eq:bethe-eigenvalue" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:bethe-eigenvalue">[eq:bethe-eigenvalue]</a>. The physical eigenvalue follows from <a href="#eq:monodromy-gauge" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:monodromy-gauge">[eq:monodromy-gauge]</a>, since $\mathcal Q_L=(-1)^n$ on the sector with $n$ minus spins.

Differentiating the left side of <a href="#eq:consecutive-counting" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:consecutive-counting">[eq:consecutive-counting]</a> with respect to every root gives <a href="#eq:Gaudin-matrix" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:Gaudin-matrix">[eq:Gaudin-matrix]</a>; the $K(0)$ term cancels the derivative of the self interaction.
